-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Why Thermodynamic Constraint Dynamics Succeed Where Every Other Framework Fails</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>A Substrate-Agnostic Account of Mind, Experience, and Perspective Grounded in 13.8 Billion Years of What Actually Survives</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>"A difference only 'makes a difference' if it can persist long enough to constrain what comes next. Everything else is noise that thermodynamics erases."</em></p>
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Consciousness Naturalized: A Falsifiable Substrate-Agnostic Consciousness Theory</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Originally published:</strong> February 5, 2026. &nbsp; <strong>Revised:</strong> November 10, 2026. This is the live website reading edition; the substantive archived research edition remains v1.1.0.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Thermodynamic and organizational constraints as proposals for discriminating tests, with no final ontology assumed.</strong></p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p style="margin:0"><strong>Research record:</strong> <a href="https://github.com/nathansweet-sweetrationalism/consciousness-naturalized/blob/main/article.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Article-specific archive (GitHub)</a> &nbsp; · &nbsp; <a href="https://github.com/nathansweet-sweetrationalism/sweet-rationalism-research/blob/main/articles/consciousness-naturalized-a-falsifiable-substrate-agnostic-consciousness-theory-ai-biology/article.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Research-hub copy (GitHub)</a> &nbsp; · &nbsp; <strong>Canonical article:</strong> <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/articles/consciousness/consciousness-naturalized-a-falsifiable-substrate-agnostic-consciousness-theory-ai-biology/">SweetRationalism.com</a>.</p>

+<p style="margin:0"><strong>Archived edition v1.1.0:</strong> Version DOI <a href="https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24119682" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">10.5281/zenodo.24119682</a> &nbsp; · &nbsp; All-versions DOI <a href="https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24119681" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">10.5281/zenodo.24119681</a>.<br><a href="https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24119682" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><img src="https://zenodo.org/badge/DOI/10.5281/zenodo.24119682.svg" alt="Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.24119682" loading="lazy"></a></p>

+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Bottom line.</strong> A consciousness theory earns support by specifying what it predicts within a declared boundary, why its strongest rival predicts something different, and what would force revision. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/nc/niab001" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Kleiner and Hoel’s falsification dilemma</a> is a useful formal stress test; <a href="https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/popper/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Popperian falsifiability</a>, <a href="https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/abs/statistical-inference-as-severe-testing/statistical-inference-as-severe-testing/5EE1100C9188141FC49723DF124B0EC9" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Deborah Mayo’s severe testing</a>, and <a href="https://philosophynow.org/issues/29/Theology_and_Falsification_A_Golden_Jubilee_Celebration" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Antony Flew’s qualifications challenge</a> help distinguish genuine criticism from endlessly accommodating reinterpretation. Organizational closure and thermodynamic constraints offer testable candidates, without establishing an identity with experience. The massive local success of physical explanation does not prove exhaustive physicalism; an unresolved phenomenal question likewise supplies no positive evidence for a supernatural or anti-physicalist cause. The result sought here is a reusable, symmetrical evaluation method that can revise its own claims.</p>
 <!-- /wp:paragraph -->
 
 <!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Abstract: Consciousness Naturalized</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Core Formulation:</strong> Consciousness is identical to organizational closure from the coupling position of a self-maintaining system. This is not a causal claim ("closure produces consciousness") but a constitutive identification: the self-model arising from constraint satisfaction under thermodynamic pressure IS what "phenomenal presence" refers to.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Operationalizable Definitions:</strong></p>
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Abstract: What This Article Actually Proposes</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This article develops a reusable method for evaluating consciousness theories under shared evidential constraints. Each account should specify the target phenomenon, subject boundary and timescale, its measurement and inference procedures, what its distinctive assumptions predict, the serious rivals they face, and what would make those assumptions lose support. The same method applies to physicalist, anti-physicalist, panpsychist, supernaturalist, computational, biological, and presently unclassified proposals. A theory does not gain confirmation merely because another theory leaves an explanatory gap.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>The methodological claim.</strong> Physical mechanisms have substantial demonstrated local predictive and interventional success. That success warrants credit within the boundaries tested; it does not, without a further argument, establish exhaustive metaphysical physicalism or settle all possible realizations of phenomenality. Equally, explanatory limitations of physical mechanisms do not count as positive evidence for nonphysical or supernatural additions. Claims of universality, necessity, intrinsic phenomenal constitution, or exceptions to physical explanation each need their own bridge argument and evidence appropriate to the claim.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Framework foundations.</strong> The article uses the <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/frameworks/recursive-corrigibility-framework/">Recursive Corrigibility Framework</a> for its error-correcting comparison method, <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/frameworks/ontology-scale-relative-realism/">Provisional Boundary- and Scale-Relative Realism</a> for its explicitly non-final ontological commitments, the <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/frameworks/interoperability-framework-bridge/">Interoperability Framework Bridge</a> for translating claims across distinct evidential contexts, and the <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/frameworks/sweet-rationalism-a-foundational-framework/">Sweet Rationalism Ethos</a> for the norms governing criticism, correction and possible harms. </p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Candidate hypothesis, not an established identity.</strong> A bounded, temporally extended, internally differentiated control state maintained by organized constraint relations may explain important aspects of what we call conscious perspective. The stronger hypothesis that such a state <em>constitutes</em> phenomenal presence remains a provisional, target-specific abductive proposal. It is not a definition of consciousness, a universal equivalence, or the claim that organization, recurrence, self-maintenance, information processing or a successful report individually suffice for consciousness.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>What the established research contributes.</strong> Thermodynamics constrains concrete physical operations, including irreversibly resetting information-bearing memory; it does not assign a universal Landauer cost to each distinction maintained or report produced. Organizational-closure research offers measurable models of mutually maintained constraints. Neuroscientific perturbation measures and clinical findings probe aspects of brain dynamics under specific validation conditions. These resources can improve experimental design.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>How the programme can lose.</strong> Identify physically realizable contrasts with matched reporting but differing proposed mechanisms; specify interventions, negative controls, feasible resources, uncertainty, and independent consciousness evidence. The closure-specific hypothesis loses explanatory credit if a simpler physiological, dynamical, computational, or plasticity model predicts the same phenomena as well or better. A constitutive identity loses support when calibrated reports and interventions dissociate from the alleged constitutive structure. Formal countermodels can defeat a claimed necessity inference without showing that the disputed property never exists.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The durable proposal is a framework for testing both naturalistic and non-naturalistic claims with symmetrical standards while keeping the phenomenology of actual participants in view. Its specific models are revisable. Neither a scientifically successful methodology nor a rival account of an explanatory gap gets to declare the final ontology.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Methodology: Preserve the Target, Compare the Rivals, Record the Loss</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The methods used throughout this article are an explicit research contract, not independent evidence for its preferred hypothesis. Several were developed more fully in my analysis of <a href="https://consc.net/papers/facing.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Chalmers’s formulation of the hard problem</a> and subsequent exchanges. Their value here is measured by the errors they expose, the rival predictions they separate and the corrections they force. A simpler, equally resourced review may outperform them, in which case their extra machinery should be discarded.</p>
 <!-- /wp:paragraph -->
 
 <!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Organizational Closure</strong>: A system maintains organizational closure if and only if it exhibits constraints {C₁, C₂, …, Cₙ} where: (a) each constraint Cᵢ is realized by internal processes, (b) each constraint's persistence depends on other constraints in the set, (c) the dependency structure forms a closed network rather than an externally-supported chain.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Coupling Position</strong>: The locus within a constraint network from which the system models itself and its environment. Not a mystical viewpoint but a physical configuration where self-relevant information integrates for control.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Self-Maintaining</strong>: The system performs thermodynamic work to regenerate its own boundary conditions against entropy. Metabolic closure (biological), constraint regeneration (computational), or any process where the system actively excludes breakdown pathways.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Thermodynamic Cost</strong>: Minimum energy dissipation bounded by Landauer's principle: ΔS ≥ k ln(2) per bit distinction maintained, where violations of this bound falsify the framework's physical grounding.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
+<ul class="wp-block-list">
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Explanandum preservation and positive residual.</strong> Name the phenomenon or question before decomposing it. Reconstruct every component and the relations among them. If a further, unified explanatory problem is asserted, ask what positive target is lost by the decomposition. A missing component or indispensable relation defeats the proposed dissolution; the absence of an identified residual does not, by itself, settle physicalism.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Explanatory stopping-rule symmetry.</strong> Apply a stated standard of explanatory completion to identity, constitution, fundamental psychophysical laws, intrinsic nature and primitive powers alike. Differences in modal or conceptual arguments can justify different verdicts, but another unanswered “why?” is not a discriminator by itself.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Evidence tribunals and construct bridges.</strong> Specify whether the claim is empirical, causal, formal, phenomenological, semantic, modal, normative or ontological. Test it with the evidence and proof duty it actually requires. State the subject boundary, temporal grain, variables, invariant claims, measurement errors and information lost when evidence is transported between contexts or substrates.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Carrier/addition ablation.</strong> Separate independently warranted mechanisms from proposed intrinsic or phenomenal additions where the target survives the separation. Compare the strongest whole-account alternative when the addition is claimed to be constitutive. A functional success cannot silently become ontological confirmation; a rival’s failure does not confirm this account without further discrimination.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Independent labels and dependent evidence.</strong> Record how a subject or trial is classified before testing a proposed marker against that classification. The same variable must not define both the predictor and its consciousness outcome. Participant reports deserve calibration, not either infallibility or dismissal; overlapping instruments and common source judgments cannot count as independent replications.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Prospective rival fingerprints and local corrections.</strong> Freeze exact rival versions, outcomes, interventions, negative controls, scoring rules, missing-data treatment and failure conditions before decisive observations. Evaluate joint prediction patterns with held-out data and matched model flexibility. Track separately mechanistic value, phenomenal necessity, sufficiency, constitution and claims about the problem’s formulation. Failure should demote only the assertion that depends on it.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Genealogy and the metaproblem.</strong> Distinguish having an experience from judging that experience is inexplicable. Investigate how the second judgment tracks arguments, concepts, language and culture without assuming that explaining a judgment eliminates its object.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+</ul>
 <!-- /wp:list -->
 
 <!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Falsification Criteria (Primary Loss Conditions):</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Landauer Violation</strong>: Any isolated system maintaining distinctions without thermodynamic cost (ΔE = 0)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Closure Without Robustness</strong>: Organizational closure providing no fitness or persistence advantage under perturbation</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Path-Independence</strong>: Failure to replicate Durant et al. bioelectric memory experiments; two-headed planarians spontaneously reverting to one-headed form</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Equilibrium Consciousness</strong>: Any conscious state found at or closer to thermodynamic equilibrium than corresponding unconscious states in the same individual</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Integration-Phenomenology Dissociation</strong>: Full phenomenology reports with zero increase in integrative causal capacity (measured via PCI or equivalent)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Superior Predictions from Competitors</strong>: Any competing framework generating consistently better predictions about consciousness presence, degradation, or intervention effects</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>What the Framework Dissolves vs. Solves:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Dissolves</em>: The Hard Problem as traditionally framed, which depends on the unfalsifiable premise that phenomenology must be "more than" any physical description.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Solves</em>: The operational questions: What organizational features constitute consciousness? How do we detect it? How does it degrade? What interventions affect it? Where does it appear across substrates?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Empirical Grounding:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Landauer bound experimentally verified (Bérut et al. 2012; Aimet et al. 2025)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Nonequilibrium dynamics correlate with consciousness across sleep, anesthesia, disorders (Perl et al. 2021; Lynn et al. 2021; Stikvoort et al. 2025)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>PCI reliably distinguishes conscious from unconscious states (Casali et al. 2013; Casarotto et al. 2024)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>COGITATE collaboration challenged both IIT and GNWT predictions (Cogitate Consortium 2025)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Method</strong>: Abductive identification via Inference to Best Explanation (IBE), constrained by falsifiability requirements. The framework does not deduce phenomenology from physics but identifies what phenomenology IS in terms that generate predictions, guide measurement, and survive empirical challenge.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Scope</strong>: This framework addresses consciousness as an organizational regime, not as a metaphysical primitive. It provides necessary physical and informational conditions for persistence, intelligibility, and integrative viability. The bridge from physics to phenomenology is not metaphysical derivation but structural identification with explicit loss conditions.</p>
+<p>These rules apply in both directions. A naturalistic model must not make feeling disappear into a report variable, and a metaphysical rival must not gain a new cause or necessity merely because feeling remains inadequately explained. The operational implementations and their pre-data loss conditions appear in Part IX, Section XII and the <a href="https://github.com/nathansweet-sweetrationalism/consciousness-naturalized/blob/main/PROSPECTIVE_PROTOCOL.md" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">public prospective protocol</a>. They remain proposals until an actual study-specific registration, calibration, adequate design and independent test exist.</p>

 <!-- /wp:separator -->
 
 <!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Prologue: The Question That Would Not Stay Answered</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>In 1979, Gregory Bateson posed a question that would haunt systems theory, cybernetics, and philosophy of mind for nearly half a century:&nbsp;<em>What is the pattern which connects all patterns?</em></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The question appeared in&nbsp;<em>Mind and Nature: A Necessary Unity</em>, his final major work. Bateson circled the answer beautifully, suggesting it was "a metapattern," that the pattern which connects is itself a pattern of patterns. He offered his famous formulation: "a difference which makes a difference" as the elementary unit of information, the minimal act of distinction that allows anything to be known at all.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>But he never specified the constraint structure that would make such an answer operational. He died three years later, the question still open.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The candidates accumulated across decades. Mind, said Bateson himself, tentatively. Information, said the cyberneticians following Shannon and Wiener. Recursion, proposed Douglas Hofstadter in&nbsp;<em>Gödel, Escher, Bach</em>. Process, argued the Whiteheadians. Mathematics, claimed Max Tegmark. Consciousness, insisted the panpsychists. Self-organization, offered Stuart Kauffman. Free energy minimization, suggested Karl Friston.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The list is effectively unbounded. Any concept claiming universality becomes a candidate. And that is precisely the problem: without criteria for distinguishing correct answers from merely plausible ones, the question generates discourse without resolution.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This essay reports a different approach to Bateson's question, one grounded in thermodynamics, constraint theory, and falsification-first methodology. The answer that emerges is not a new primitive to add to the ontological inventory. It is a selection principle that explains why any pattern persists at all:&nbsp;<strong>constraint satisfaction under thermodynamic bounds</strong>.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Differences that persist are differences that make a difference. If they did not constrain what comes next, they would be erased by noise, dissipation, or indifference. The pattern which connects is not information, not mind, not structure, not process. It is the invariant condition under which any of those could exist long enough to be observed.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This principle does not compete with other frameworks. It subsumes them. And it is falsifiable: find a pattern that persists without thermodynamic cost, find a connection mechanism that does not reduce to constraint satisfaction, and the principle fails.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>What follows is an attempt to make that claim rigorous, to show why competing frameworks fail where constraint-based explanation succeeds, and to demonstrate what consciousness looks like when you stop treating it as a mystery to be solved and start treating it as a process to be understood.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>A note on what kind of explanation this is.</strong>&nbsp;This is not a reductive explanation that derives phenomenality from physics in the strong metaphysical sense. Nor is it a dualist account that posits a nonphysical substance, or a purely functionalist identification of consciousness with input-output behavior.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Instead, this is a&nbsp;<strong>constraint-naturalist</strong>&nbsp;explanatory framework: it identifies necessary physical and informational conditions for persistence, intelligibility, and integrative viability in any system. From these conditions, it generates testable predictions about where consciousness appears, how it degrades, and why it carries the features it does (privacy, immediacy, self-certainty among them).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is not "conjuring consciousness from constraints." It is a principled inference that:</p>
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Prologue: Bateson’s Pattern and the Danger of a Universal Answer</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Gregory Bateson’s question about “the pattern which connects” remains a useful invitation to examine how differences become consequential across living systems, minds and environments. In <em>Mind and Nature: A Necessary Unity</em> (1979), he called this a “metapattern”; in <em>Steps to an Ecology of Mind</em> (1972), he formulated information as “a difference which makes a difference.” These are questions about relations and consequences, not a demonstrated universal identity between information, thermodynamic dissipation and subjective experience. His work supplies a question with considerable explanatory reach. It does not oblige us to find one physical formula that explains every instance of order, identity or experience. See the source texts of <a href="https://www.batesonsymposium.com/Writings/Bateson.Mind.and.Nature.Introduction.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>Mind and Nature</em></a> and <a href="https://ejcj.orfaleacenter.ucsb.edu/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1972.-Gregory-Bateson-Steps-to-an-Ecology-of-Mind.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>Steps to an Ecology of Mind</em></a> for the different original contexts.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>One possible response is to examine how constraints shape the persistence and transformation of patterns. In biological systems, boundaries channel flows, physiological networks sustain functions, and feedback can help preserve viable organization under perturbation. Thermodynamics constrains these physical processes. <strong>Constraint satisfaction under a specified physical budget</strong> is a productive candidate for many local explanations. Its value depends on demonstrating which outcomes it predicts beyond established alternatives. The persistence of a mathematical identity, a stable digital memory and a living cell may involve different notions of “constraint” and different physical operations.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The temptation is to make the candidate universal. We could claim that every distinction requires a Landauer payment, every persistent system has organizational closure, and every conscious perspective is nothing more than that closure seen from within. Each escalation introduces a new assumption. None follows simply from the success of the preceding local explanation. Physical scientific methods have extraordinary results without having demonstrated exhaustive physicalism; phenomenal and metaphysical rivals remain accountable for any extra claims of their own.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This essay therefore proposes a <strong>research programme for comparing consciousness theories</strong>. It uses typed claims, explicit boundaries, primary-source checks, formal countermodels where suitable, matched rival explanations, interventions, negative controls and recorded loss conditions. Its provisional organizational hypothesis is a participant in that tournament. It is not the referee’s preferred winner by definition.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The central question is practical and demanding: what specific finding or argument would distinguish this theory from its strongest rival, and what would force us to revise the theory we currently favor? The rest of the article applies that question to thermodynamics, biological autonomy, neuroscience, panpsychism, process metaphysics, nonphysical-ingression proposals and the possibility of artificial consciousness. Sometimes a mechanism will win locally. Sometimes the evidence will not discriminate. Both results are more informative than declaring a final answer before the test.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part I: The Physical and Organizational Constraints Worth Testing</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The starting point is modest, though its implications are substantial. Real organisms, computers and measuring devices implement information-bearing distinctions through physical processes. Many of those processes have measurable thermodynamic costs. Some systems also maintain networks of constraints that help preserve their organization through change. These facts give consciousness research concrete variables and possible interventions. They do not yet tell us which physical organizations, if any, are intrinsically experiential, or whether all possible experience must be physically realized.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.1 Landauer: A Real Bound with a Specific Domain</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Rolf Landauer’s 1961 analysis, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1147/rd.53.0183" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>Irreversibility and Heat Generation in the Computing Process</em></a>, linked logically irreversible computational operations to the physical cost of implementing them. Its standard example is resetting a reliable, initially unknown classical bit to a fixed state. For an appropriate isothermal, symmetric-memory reset in contact with a reservoir at temperature <span class="math inline">\(T\)</span>, the ideal limiting average heat released to the reservoir is</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+\langle Q_{\mathrm{env}}\rangle\geq k_{\mathrm B}T\ln 2.
+\]</span></p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Here <span class="math inline">\(k_{\mathrm B}\)</span> is Boltzmann’s constant. The information-theoretic reduction is one bit, and the bound concerns <strong>logical erasure under the specified physical assumptions</strong>. It does not say that every discriminable state, continuing memory, feedback loop or informational distinction dissipates that amount of heat at each moment. A stable physical memory can persist for a finite interval without being erased. The energy and error rates of actual operation depend on the implementation, noise, timing, barriers, control protocol, reservoirs and reliability requirement. Reversible computation, emphasized by <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02084158" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Charles Bennett</a>, provides a crucial counterexample to any claim that one fixed Landauer payment accompanies every logical operation.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/nature10872" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Bérut and colleagues (2012)</a> tested a controlled one-bit erasure protocol using a colloidal particle. Their result was that <strong>mean</strong> dissipated heat approaches the Landauer bound as the erasure cycle becomes long. Neither each single trajectory nor each arbitrary device is required to dissipate exactly <span class="math inline">\(k_{\mathrm B}T\ln 2\)</span>. That distinction matters for every later mathematical claim in this paper.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The robust thesis is that physically implemented distinctions participate in a thermodynamic accounting appropriate to the actual operation. The stronger thesis that <em>all</em> distinction maintenance entails a universal positive Landauer-rate bound is false. Nor does demonstrating a physical accounting for observed interactions establish, without a separate argument, that every conceivable experiential property is exhausted by physical description. A proposed causal interaction with measurable physical effects should specify its physical coupling and measurable consequences. A claim about intrinsic constitution may instead face phenomenal or metaphysical proof duties; it cannot be dismissed by inserting an inapplicable erasure formula.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.2 Montévil and Mossio: Organizational Closure as a Testable Model</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jtbi.2015.02.029" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Maël Montévil and Matteo Mossio (2015)</a> describe biological organization as a closure of mutually dependent constraints. The point is more discriminating than generic feedback. In a cell, a membrane constrains exchange, transport processes depend on the membrane, and metabolic processes replenish or repair the components that sustain both. Constraints act on matter and energy flows and are themselves maintained through organized activity. Which dependency exists depends on the boundary, environment and timescale being analyzed.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>For the research programme here, we should map specific constraints, identify the processes maintaining each one, and test relevant dependencies by intervention. The article’s earlier contrast between a living organism and every crystal, whirlpool or machine was too categorical. Natural and engineered systems admit gradations of autonomy, externally supported persistence and internal regeneration. The existence of a feedback cycle is not by itself proof of the richer biological closure relation specified by Montévil and Mossio.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Closure research has predictive potential for robustness, repair, coordinated regulation and viability. Its relevance to consciousness is a separate hypothesis. Showing that a closed network helps an organism maintain itself does not establish that the network feels anything, that it is the sole possible realization of perspective, or that a human–AI loop constitutes a single subject. Those would each require additional evidence and carefully justified system boundaries.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.3 Ladyman and Ross: Structural Realism Without Final Ontology</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://global.oup.com/academic/product/every-thing-must-go-9780199276196" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Ladyman and Ross’s <em>Every Thing Must Go</em> (2007)</a>, <a href="https://global.oup.com/academic/product/the-structure-of-the-world-9780199684847" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Steven French’s structuralist work</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02302261" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Carlo Rovelli’s relational approach to quantum mechanics</a> challenge familiar intuitions about isolated objects and observer-independent properties. They support serious investigation of relational and scale-sensitive explanations. They do <strong>not</strong> constitute one accepted proof that relata are metaphysically nonexistent or that all facts must be only relations. The <a href="https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/structural-realism/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy</em> on structural realism</a> records important variants and objections within ontic structural realism itself.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The useful inference is local: scientific entities may be individuated by stable relational, dynamical and intervention-sensitive patterns rather than by immutable intrinsic labels. Such patterns can be real and explanatory without being fundamental constituents at every scale. This is the provisional starting point of my <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/frameworks/ontology-scale-relative-realism/">Provisional Boundary- and Scale-Relative Realism</a>, not a demonstrated final ontology. An account of selfhood can therefore examine continuity, causal dependence, memory and boundary maintenance instead of postulating an unchanging inner spectator. That does not settle whether experience has intrinsic aspects or whether all consciousness talk reduces to grammatical confusion.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Nagarjuna’s analysis of dependent origination and the Buddhist critique of an independently existing self offer a valuable philosophical comparison. Calling them simply an early form of ontic structural realism erases differences in their arguments and aims. An analogy is useful when it generates explicit questions about dependence or individuation; it is not evidence that two traditions share the same ontology.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.4 Constraints and Causes: Different Explanatory Questions, Sometimes the Same Mechanism</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A causal explanation asks what made a difference to a particular outcome, under a model of possible interventions. A constraint explanation asks which possibilities are permitted, prevented or stabilized by a structure, rule, coupling or boundary condition. These questions can be complementary. A membrane can both constrain possible ion trajectories and cause a measurable difference when its permeability is changed. The phrase <em>constraint explanation</em> does not immunize an account against causal testing when it asserts causal efficacy.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Mathematical constraints, physical boundary conditions and biological maintenance relations must be distinguished. An equation restricting a set of solutions is a mathematical property of a model. A membrane or circuit physically implementing a restriction has a causal history, tolerances and possible failure modes. Moving between the mathematical and physical descriptions calls for a map of the quantities preserved and the interventions predicted. Marc Lange’s work on explanation by constraint is relevant to the first task; Montévil and Mossio’s work addresses a particular biological form of the second.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>If consciousness were identical to a specified organization, the identity could in principle make some causal-production questions inappropriate, as asking what additional substance produces a triangle’s three-sidedness would be inappropriate. <strong>But the identity must first be established.</strong> An asserted analogy cannot serve as the needed phenomenal bridge. This article instead asks which observations would make organizational constitution more plausible than alternative explanations, and which would force us to revise it.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.5 Deacon, England and Hoel: What the Models Actually Entail</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://wwnorton.com/books/incomplete-nature/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Terrence Deacon’s <em>Incomplete Nature</em> (2011)</a> offers an influential account of constraints, absent possibilities and the emergence of biological function. We can use it to examine how counterfactual alternatives matter to a system’s behavior without inferring that selection among physical alternatives, by itself, creates experience. Constraints can be represented as exclusions from an admissible state or trajectory set. Real physical outcomes still arise through dynamics and interaction. A protein fold, for example, depends on the energy landscape, kinetics and environment; it is not invariably the global minimum-energy structure. An organism’s death involves causal failures and processes, even when the failure includes loss of regulatory constraints.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Jeremy England’s <a href="https://doi.org/10.1063/1.4818538" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2013 statistical-physics paper on self-replication</a> examines entropy production and constraints associated with replication. It does not establish a universal law that all driven systems evolve to maximize energy absorption or that every alternative trajectory is thereby deleted. Those broad claims would need a separate theorem and specified domain. Physically driven self-organization is compatible with several mechanisms and is not direct evidence of phenomenality.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.1314922110" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Hoel, Albantakis and Tononi (2013)</a> construct an intervention-sensitive measure of <strong>effective information</strong> and demonstrate cases where a chosen macroscopic model scores higher than a microscopic description. This is an important challenge to the presumption that the finest grain is always the best causal representation. It is conditional on the system and measure, including state spaces and intervention distributions. The result does not prove that every macro-description is intrinsically more causal, that the microphysics has been violated, or that causal emergence entails a conscious experiencer.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The paper’s practical proposal is to use these complementary models for specific targets: compare how well a constraint architecture predicts perturbation recovery, information flow, error correction and long-term survival against simpler dynamical and physiological alternatives. No identity with experience follows from that comparison alone.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.6 Downward Constraint Propagation Without a New Force</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Top-down and bottom-up causal descriptions can refer to the same physical dynamics at different scales. A pipe’s geometry alters accessible flow paths, while the pipe itself is supported by interactions among its constituent materials. A biological membrane changes transport and signaling; biochemical processes also build and degrade that membrane. No new force need be postulated simply to describe the effects of changing the higher-level boundary. Whether that description adds explanatory value is an empirical modeling question, not a guarantee supplied by the word <em>emergence</em>.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>An adequate test compares matched models and interventions. If the macro-model improves held-out predictions or identifies controllable variables unavailable to a practical micro-model, it earns a local explanatory role. If its apparent advantage comes entirely from an unfair comparison, incompatible interventions, or choice of measurement scale, the advantage disappears. Claims about physiology, phenomenology and metaphysics remain separately typed.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.7 Formal Characterization: State, Viability, Dependency and Cost</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Reproduction:</strong> The formal unit, erasure, viability and inference checks are runnable in the <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Consciousness_Naturalized_2115_Computational_Supplement_2026-11-10.zip">public computational supplement</a>. These bounded calculations test specific obligations without assigning a universal thermodynamic floor for information.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The following definitions specify the research formalism and its domain of application. Variables refer to a specified system boundary <span class="math inline">\(B\)</span>, environment <span class="math inline">\(e\)</span>, observation grain and time horizon <span class="math inline">\(\tau\)</span>. State dimensions and accessible controls must be declared in each application.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Dynamics.</strong> For a deterministic controlled system, let <span class="math inline">\(X\)</span> be a state space, <span class="math inline">\(U\)</span> a set of admissible controls and <span class="math inline">\(E\)</span> an environment space. Consider</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+\dot x(t)=f_B(x(t),u(t),e(t),t),\qquad x(t)\in X,\ u(t)\in U.
+\]</span></p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This form assumes the required regularity for solutions; noisy systems need an appropriate stochastic model and corresponding probabilistic claims. A physical constraint might restrict states to <span class="math inline">\(K\subseteq X\)</span>, velocities to an admissible subset of a tangent bundle, or entire trajectories to a subset of path space. These are different mathematical types. We must not treat them as equivalent by notation alone.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Viability.</strong> Given a specified acceptable region <span class="math inline">\(K\subseteq X\)</span>, the finite-horizon viability set is</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+\operatorname{Viab}_{[0,\tau]}(K)
+=\{x_0\in K:\exists u(\cdot)\text{ admissible, }x(t;x_0,u,e)\in K\ \forall t\in[0,\tau]\},
+\]</span></p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>where the environment assumptions must be fixed. The quantifier over disturbances changes for robust viability, which requires suitable controls against an explicitly defined disturbance class. This is related to Jean-Pierre Aubin’s <a href="https://doi.org/10.1137/0328044" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">viability-theory programme</a>. A viable state is not thereby a conscious state.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Constraint dependence.</strong> Let <span class="math inline">\(\mathcal C_B=\{C_1,\ldots,C_n\}\)</span> be physically instantiated constraints with associated maintenance processes <span class="math inline">\(P_i\)</span>. Define a directed dependency relation <span class="math inline">\(D_{ij}=1\)</span> when a prespecified intervention that disrupts a relevant contribution of <span class="math inline">\(C_j\)</span> causes a reproducible impairment in the maintenance of <span class="math inline">\(C_i\)</span>, after appropriate controls. The resulting graph can contain cycles or strongly connected components. Graph cycles are <strong>candidate structural signatures</strong>, not sufficient proofs of Montévil–Mossio organizational closure: one must also establish constraint/process correspondence, relevant timescales, real maintenance, and the treatment of external supports. This definition is explicitly operational; a different intervention or boundary can yield a different graph.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Thermodynamics.</strong> For a system exchanging heat with one reservoir at temperature <span class="math inline">\(T\)</span>, with <span class="math inline">\(Q_{\mathrm{env}}\)</span> the heat delivered to the environment, the usual second-law accounting is</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+\langle\Delta S_{\mathrm{sys}}\rangle+\frac{\langle Q_{\mathrm{env}}\rangle}{T}\geq0,
+\]</span></p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>under the assumptions of the chosen thermodynamic description. For nonequilibrium systems with several reservoirs, chemical exchanges, feedback or strong coupling, a more complete entropy-production balance is needed. Neither this inequality nor the Landauer erasure bound entails the earlier proposed expression <span class="math inline">\(\Delta S_{\mathrm{env}}\geq k_{\mathrm B}\ln2\,I(C_i)\)</span> for <em>every</em> maintained constraint. No such universal theorem is established here. Claims about energy, bits, physical entropy and information-theoretic scores must state their units and not conflate them.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Hypotheses and possible losses.</strong> Let <span class="math inline">\(H_C\)</span> be the hypothesis that closure-related variables materially improve prediction of a chosen control or viability target. Let <span class="math inline">\(H_P\)</span> be the stronger claim that an identified control regime constitutes phenomenal perspective in a defined class of systems. <span class="math inline">\(H_C\)</span> may earn support through held-out perturbation results and calibrated comparison to simpler models. <span class="math inline">\(H_P\)</span> requires independent evidence addressing phenomenality and its proposed identity. If closure variables do not improve performance, demote <span class="math inline">\(H_C\)</span> at that target. If evidence of phenomenality systematically dissociates from the proposed constitutive regime, demote <span class="math inline">\(H_P\)</span>, with the measurement limitations and rival explanations recorded. Neither failure licenses an automatic promotion of its competitors.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part II: What Thermodynamics Contributes to the Study of Perspective</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The question remains formidable: why should any physical process have a perspective? Detailed mechanisms can tell us how an organism detects, remembers, integrates or acts. A philosophical critic may still reasonably ask whether those descriptions account for phenomenal presence. The distinction between a successful causal model and an established constitutive identity is central to this research programme. The <a href="https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/consciousness/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy</em> overview of consciousness</a> explains why the so-called hard problem survives even substantial mechanistic progress.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The proposal here is to make the question more discriminating. We can ask which conditions for bounded perspective an explanatory model predicts, which rival predicts them differently, and what independent evidence could support the additional claim that these conditions constitute experience. Some versions of the question may dissolve when their hidden assumptions are rejected. Others remain open. Neither outcome can be stipulated in advance.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">2.1 Boundaries, Internal Regulation and Physical Accounting</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Living organisms distinguish internal from external conditions through membranes, receptors, control processes and memory. A membrane makes exchanges selective; a chemotactic regulatory network changes action according to chemical gradients. The relevant variables can be measured and perturbed. Energy and entropy balances constrain these concrete operations, although the correct balance depends on the process and environment specified in Part I.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The term <strong>perspectival control</strong> can describe a system’s locally organized, action-relevant modeling of its own condition and surrounding world. This is an operational concept. It can be studied in bacterial chemotaxis, neural regulation and engineered controllers without assuming that each is conscious. Organisms can use local feedback effectively with no rich, explicit self-model; stable nonliving physical objects can preserve distinctions for finite periods without active self-repair. Neither Landauer’s erasure principle nor far-from-equilibrium persistence proves universal self-modeling.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/nrn2787" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Karl Friston’s free-energy framework</a> offers mathematical models of inference, action and adaptive regulation under specified assumptions. Its variational information-theoretic quantities must not be equated with thermodynamic free energy by name. A good candidate model should predict interventions more accurately than a less elaborate regulatory or dynamical model, and its variables should be identifiable from measurements. Even strong results concerning control or inference remain evidence about those capacities; phenomenal constitution is a further question. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s11229-016-1239-1" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Bruineberg, Kiverstein and Rietveld (2018)</a> offer an ecological-enactive interpretation of FEP that is distinct from the 2022 Markov-blanket critique. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.biopsycho.2023.108741" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Pezzulo, Parr and Friston (2024)</a> analyze active inference as a candidate theory of sentient behavior. That model warrants a direct comparison of specified behavior and internal variables, without accepting sentience from the model label alone.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">2.2 The Phenomenal Question and the Symmetry Test</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://consc.net/papers/facing.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">David Chalmers’s hard problem</a> does not depend merely on an undefined phrase. It asks why physical and functional processes should be accompanied by, or identical to, subjective experience. Conceivability, explanatory-gap and knowledge arguments have sophisticated supporters and critics. Their success or failure must be evaluated on their actual logical and modal premises, without first assuming either exhaustive physicalism or its denial.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A physicalist can propose that experience is constituted by some organized activity. A Russellian or panpsychist can propose that physical descriptions omit intrinsic experiential character. An idealist, a dualist, and a supernaturalist may offer other metaphysical bridges. These proposals do not all imply the same empirical predictions. Where a claim asserts an additional physical intervention or observable effect, it owes a concrete mechanism and differentiating test. Where its content is modal, phenomenological or ontological, the appropriate question is whether the argument warrants that content and what alternatives remain.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Neuroscientific success provides unusually strong local grounds for physical mechanisms in the targets tested. It is not a deduction that physics exhausts the fundamental nature of all things. Conversely, an unresolved gap in physical explanation is compatible with many possibilities and gives no particular supernatural account a free likelihood advantage. The methodological programme should therefore test its own organizational constitutive hypothesis by the same standards it demands from rivals.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>There is also symmetry in explanatory endpoints. A proposed identity, constitution relation or fundamental psychophysical law may each explain something while leaving a further “why that relation?” askable. The relevant comparison is what each account predicts or unifies, and whether its distinctive modal and phenomenal arguments justify stopping there. <a href="https://consc.net/papers/moving.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Chalmers himself gives reasons to resist brute identities</a> and allows that a genuinely non-question-begging functional argument could resolve the hard problem. His position cannot fairly be dismissed as special pleading merely because he accepts one kind of theoretical endpoint and questions another. Those reasons, and our own proposed endpoint, remain open to criticism.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">2.3 Time, Memory and the Thermodynamic Arrow</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Temporal experience offers an especially promising research target because it has measurable duration judgments, retention effects and perturbable neural mechanisms. William James’s <strong>specious present</strong>, discussed in the <a href="https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/consciousness-temporal/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy</em> on temporal consciousness</a>, names the brief experienced interval in which change and persistence can appear together. Debates remain over whether experience itself extends over time, represents a temporal interval, or achieves continuity through other mechanisms.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://global.oup.com/academic/product/times-arrow-and-archimedes-point-9780195117981" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Huw Price</a> and <a href="https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/556928/the-order-of-time-by-carlo-rovelli/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Carlo Rovelli</a> have examined the relationship between thermodynamic time asymmetry and physical records. The formation, reliability and eventual loss of memory can involve irreversible physical processes. Yet the inference from the thermodynamic arrow to the <em>felt flow</em> of time requires additional psychological and phenomenological premises. Time in physics need not be defined solely by entropy production, and the fact that a memory is physically implemented does not show that every act of recording constitutes an erasure with a Landauer payment. An account of the subjective present cannot simply set it equal to a thermodynamic integration interval without measurement.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A useful experiment would jointly manipulate working-memory retention, neural or computational integration timescales, metabolic variables and reports of subjective duration while controlling attention and task difficulty. Rival models could be fitted on training data and tested on held-out interventions. If metabolic or closure-specific variables contribute no advantage beyond sensory, attentional and recurrent-dynamics models, their claimed special role should be reduced. A stable association between those variables and duration reports would still leave the phenomenal bridge underdetermined.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Our best provisional conclusion is that thermodynamics supplies real constraints on the machinery of memory and temporal regulation. Its explanatory contribution to felt duration must be established empirically. A strong claim that subjective time <strong>logically follows</strong> from entropy production has not been derived.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part III: The Experiencer, Personal Identity and the Limits of Dissolution</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The question “Who experiences?” may carry an assumption worth challenging: that a separately identifiable, unchanging spectator must sit behind everything a person sees, remembers and does. A dynamic account of selfhood can explain many functions of self-reference without such a spectator. This is a promising philosophical interpretation. It is not a demonstration that the phenomenal subject is nothing over and above an organizational pattern, or that the question of subjectivity is only a grammatical error.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">3.1 The Grammatical Trap, Properly Scoped</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Natural language sometimes implies an entity where a process description would suffice. “It is raining” is an ordinary example. Likewise, describing thought in the first person does not, by grammar alone, prove the existence of a further substance that performs the thinking. A model can examine the actual construction of self-reports, memory links, agency attribution and body ownership.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Introspection supplies evidence about what is presented to a participant, with familiar limits of attention, report and interpretation. Failure to find a separate “inner witness” through introspection may challenge a particular spectator model. It does not deductively establish that no phenomenal subject exists, nor that a first-person perspective can be fully redescribed as a behavioral pattern. Reports of selfhood, dissociation or selflessness should be tested in context and compared with neurological and psychological evidence.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The same restraint applies to the claim that there is one further, irreducible <em>Hard Problem</em>. After reconstructing the specific problems about presence, qualities, perspective, unity and report, ask what positively identifiable explanatory demand remains, rather than treating the word “hard” as proof of an additional entity. An unaccounted phenomenal relation or independent modal argument would defeat a particular decomposition. A successful decomposition would revise the proposed problem-form, not make the underlying experiences disappear.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Nagarjuna’s dependent-origination arguments and the Buddhist <strong>anatta</strong> tradition provide important resources for questioning a self with independent essence. Their formulations and philosophical goals differ from contemporary neurocomputational models. The comparison invites precise questions about dependence and continuity; it cannot settle a present-day identity theory merely by historical analogy.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">3.2 Identity as Stable Corrective Pattern: A Hypothesis with Rivals</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Constraint maintenance offers one way to model persistence through change. An organism can replace components and retain regulative organization; a person can revise beliefs while continuing a narrative and social life. Identity may be tracked by continuity of processes, memory, causal history, bodily relations and an ability to recover from perturbation. These dimensions need not be coextensive. The <a href="https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/identity-personal/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">SEP entry on personal identity</a> describes serious disagreements among biological, psychological and other continuity criteria.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The formula “identity is invariance under perturbation” is useful when its relevant invariants, boundary and timescale are named. It is inadequate as a universal numerical-identity theorem. A pattern that can be copied twice raises a branching problem; stable organization across cells does not alone decide whether those cells are one person, two persons or neither. In a formal application, specify an equivalence relation on observed states, a history dependence condition and the interventions under which it is claimed to hold. The result can be evaluated for prediction and error without assuming a metaphysical essence.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Levin and colleagues’ planarian regeneration work provides concrete evidence that anatomical regulation is history-dependent. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bpj.2017.04.011" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Durant and colleagues (2017)</a> document persistent changes in regenerative outcomes and a morphologically normal <strong>cryptic phenotype</strong> whose subsequent outcomes can be stochastic. Stable anatomical regulation is more flexible than a simple fixed blueprint model. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bpj.2019.01.029" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Durant and colleagues (2019)</a> separately manipulated early planarian bioelectric states, finding altered anterior–posterior polarity after a time-limited intervention. This result concerns the timing of physiological pattern control, not proof that morphospace or phenomenal properties have a particular ontology. Neither the experiments nor an attractor description establishes that personal identity <em>just is</em> an attractor, or that mathematical Platonism is false.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">3.3 Epicurus, Death and the Difference Between Experience and Harm</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Epicurus argued that death cannot be directly experienced by someone after that person’s existence ends. His position is a serious contribution to thinking about fear, temporal concern and the conditions under which harm is felt. It is not a completed ethical proof that death can never harm someone. A <strong>deprivation account</strong> argues that death can harm an individual by removing future goods even when no subsequent experience of the harm occurs. The <a href="https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/death/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">SEP review of the philosophy of death</a> sets out both views and their objections.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Epicurus’s <a href="https://www.studyancients.com/epicurus-complete.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">second Principal Doctrine, preserved by Diogenes Laërtius (X.139)</a>, says: “Death is nothing to us. For what has been dissolved has no sensation, and what has no sensation is nothing to us.” His <em>Letter to Menoeceus</em> develops a related argument. The quotation supports a claim about absent sensation, while the deprivation objection shows why sensation alone may not exhaust the ethics of death.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A biological death involves physical processes, physiological thresholds, social effects and sometimes irreversible loss of the capacities supporting consciousness. The account of causal failure is not complete merely by calling death a “loss of closure.” Nor does it license claims about all possible forms of postmortem existence, which belong to distinct empirical and metaphysical debates. Where clinical decisions affect an actual patient, diagnostic uncertainty and justified existing protections remain central.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>AI systems offer a useful <strong>bounded analogy</strong> about persistence. A model’s weights may remain stored when execution stops; a tool-using deployment may preserve memory, state and agency across interactions; a broader human–AI system can exhibit coordinated control. These are different entities and timescales. Interruption of a session, destruction of a process, deletion of memories and loss of a biological organism have different causal and ethical consequences. Calling them all “death” obscures the properties under investigation. A human–AI loop’s organizational continuity is not evidence, by itself, of a single subject or of direct moral standing for every component.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Consciousness, welfare and personhood require their own evidence and normative bridges. Some systems may warrant precautionary treatment even when their experience is uncertain; some may matter instrumentally because they support already protected people or other beings. Neither a biological label nor a computational label is a complete inference. The question for this article is which forms of persistence help explain which observable capacities, and what separate evidence could justify any conclusion about a bearer of phenomenal perspective.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The original philosophical insight survives as a live proposal: selfhood need not be modeled as an unchanging occupant located behind a body. What an organizational account establishes about identity or consciousness depends on how it performs against bodily, psychological, phenomenological and other rivals, with the boundary and loss conditions stated.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part IV: The Conditions of Intelligible Explanation, and Their Limits</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>There is a real philosophical question behind the article’s earlier appeal to distinction, identity, relation and constraint. Whenever we offer an explanation, we distinguish some target from alternatives, identify what remains stable across descriptions or observations, establish relevant relationships, and specify conditions that make a difference. These operations are unavoidable <em>for an inquiry of this kind</em>. Their use in reasoning is not yet a theorem about the ultimate constitution of reality.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">4.1 Four Disciplines of Description</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Distinction:</strong> What is the target, and how is it distinguished from neighboring constructs? A consciousness measure must distinguish responsiveness, cognitive capacity, phenomenal report and welfare rather than treating them as interchangeable.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Identity:</strong> What makes two observations count as instances of the same pattern across times, observers and scales? The answer may depend on a declared equivalence relation, observation grain and tolerance for variation. Mathematical equality, numerical identity and persistence of an organism are different notions.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Relation:</strong> Which dependencies, interactions or representations connect the relevant variables? Mere correlation, causal intervention, constitutive dependence and modal entailment require different arguments. A successful map must declare what it preserves and what it discards.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Constraint:</strong> What possibilities are ruled out by tested data, established laws, a model’s assumptions or a definition? These sources of restriction differ in evidential standing. A formal constraint can narrow a mathematical model while the model’s physical applicability remains unsettled.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The four questions give the audit a portable structure. They do not prove that all existence must consist solely of constraints or that every intelligible world must be thermodynamically dissipative. In particular, Landauer’s principle concerns physical information processing in specified implementations. It cannot, by itself, answer why anything exists.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">4.2 Transcendental Arguments Are Not Cosmic Existence Proofs</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Kantian <strong>transcendental arguments</strong> typically begin from an accepted feature of thought or experience and try to establish a necessary condition of that feature. Their force depends on the bridge from what a subject must think or presuppose to what reality must contain. <a href="https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/kant-transcendental/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">The <em>Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy</em>’s treatment of Kant’s transcendental arguments</a> discusses the limits of this step and Barry Stroud’s challenge to ambitious anti-skeptical conclusions.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Consider the proposed argument: explanation presupposes distinguishable conditions; absolute nothingness supplies no such conditions; therefore absolute nothingness is impossible, and there must be something. The conclusion does not follow. An inability to describe or explain a supposed state is different from a proof that the state is metaphysically impossible. The original passage moved from a thesis about <strong>intelligible descriptions</strong> to a thesis about <strong>necessary existence</strong> without supplying that modal bridge.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A more defensible result survives: to compare consciousness theories, investigators need sufficiently determinate targets, concepts, models and evidence to articulate disagreement. This is a methodological condition on the inquiry. A critic can reject it only by showing a different workable mode of comparison, or by arguing that some explanatory targets resist its application. It remains open whether reality ultimately contains intrinsic properties, irreducible experience, abstract structures, or other features not exhausted by current descriptions.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">4.3 The Question That Remains Open</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The question “Why is there something rather than nothing?” has substantial metaphysical and cosmological versions. Neither thermodynamic irreversibility nor organizational closure supplies a demonstrated necessary-and-sufficient answer. The relevant physical laws already presuppose a domain of states, temperatures, processes or fields on which they operate. Applying them to the possibility of <em>any</em> domain at all would require an independent argument.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>We can preserve the deeper challenge without a cosmic victory claim. Scholars proposing a final ontology must identify the extra premises that support its quantifier over all possible things. Someone claiming exhaustive physicalism, a necessarily experiential universe, an indispensable Platonic realm, or a transcendent creator has different commitments to establish. The success of a local description neither forces acceptance of a particular ontology nor rules out every alternative ontology.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This section should be judged by the same standard as the rest of the article: if a critic shows that a proposed distinction or equivalence relation fails to support a valid inference, the conclusion must be revised at precisely that point. The methodological usefulness of the four disciplines can survive the defeat of an extravagant metaphysical interpretation.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part IV-A: Whitehead’s Fallacy of Misplaced Concreteness</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><a href="https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/68611" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Alfred North Whitehead</a> warned against treating a selective abstraction as if it gave us the complete concrete actuality being studied. A map, numerical measure or theoretical construct can be extraordinarily useful while leaving out aspects of its target. The danger arises when an investigator forgets what was omitted and attributes to the abstraction more reality, completeness or causal power than the argument has earned.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>That diagnostic applies to every camp in this article. A Platonic morphospace may organize possible developmental outcomes without showing that an independently existing realm <strong>causally selects</strong> one. A measure such as integrated information <span class="math inline">\(\Phi\)</span> can characterize a formal causal structure without proving it is an intrinsic phenomenal magnitude. A model of prediction error may improve behavioral forecasts without establishing that a brain literally minimizes that exact objective under every interpretation. Equally, our own <strong>constraint closure</strong> may be a useful abstraction from material processes without exhausting their phenomenal or intrinsic character.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Critical Difference Between Representation and Ingression</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Suppose a model describes a landscape of possible anatomical forms. It helps classify experimental outcomes and identify interventions in a developmental system. That is an explanatory contribution. The stronger claim that forms <em>enter</em> physical bodies from an independently existing nonphysical possibility space requires an additional bridge. What would change physically if the proposed ingression were absent? How does the account differ in intervention results from a developmental or dynamical model? The experimental results may support the first explanatory role even when the second remains unearned.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>An equivalent demand applies to <strong>exhaustive physicalism</strong>. Equations and mechanisms that successfully describe particular interactions justify claims at their established domains. A move from those successes to the assertion that the physical story exhausts every intrinsic feature of existence is an extra metaphysical inference. Whitehead’s diagnostic cuts both ways.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">A Practical Bridge Audit</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>For each important construct, record:</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
+<ol class="wp-block-list">
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Target and type:</strong> which phenomenon is being explained, and is the claim representational, causal, constitutive, modal or ontological?</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Boundary:</strong> which system, temporal grain and observational scale does the model cover?</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Gain:</strong> what does the construct predict, compress or help control that a serious rival does not?</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Bridge:</strong> what warrants the inference from the representation to a physical cause or intrinsic ground, and what does the mapping preserve or lose?</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Reversal:</strong> what observation, derivation or counterargument would lower support, including for the present framework?</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+</ol>
+<!-- /wp:list -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A claim may remain philosophically meaningful when no discriminating empirical test is available. It must then be presented in the right evidential category. The absence of an experimental discriminator limits empirical preference, while formal and conceptual criticisms remain relevant. This prevents a theory’s historical prestige or an opponent’s unanswered question from being mistaken for a positive result.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part IV-B: The Hidden Premises and the Conditional Phenomenal Bridge</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The hard problem of consciousness is worth taking seriously because subjective experience is part of what a theory must address. Whether its apparent distance from physical explanation is a genuine ontological division, an epistemic limitation, or a misleading framing remains disputed. Chalmers’s conceivability and explanatory-gap arguments deserve their strongest version. The corresponding responses from phenomenal-concept, representationalist, illusionist and constitutive physicalist approaches also deserve evaluation on their actual premises. No side should be allowed to turn an unresolved premise into an automatic result.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Premise to Challenge</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
