Where they lack identifiability, adequate controls, power analysis or a suitable indicator of phenomenal presence, the correct audit result is <strong>not yet discriminating</strong>.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">9.6 Research Protocols: Discriminating Proposals, Not Completed Tests</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The protocols below are <em>candidate designs</em>. They have not been preregistered, powered, ethically approved, or shown to isolate phenomenal experience from every relevant alternative. A quantitative prediction becomes scientifically defensible only after its parameters, comparator, measurement error and possible failure criterion are fixed independently of the outcome. We should separate tests of organizational control, tests of consciousness indicators, and tests of the proposed constitutive bridge.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.6.1 Cross-scale Causal Propagation</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Target:</strong> whether a measured graph of physical constraint dependencies predicts perturbation spread beyond a matched model of ordinary physical coupling.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Proposed design:</strong> estimate dependencies in a system with an independently characterized architecture; intervene on selected nodes where lawful and safe; record local and downstream responses at predefined time lags. Compare a constraint-maintenance graph, a dynamical connectivity model and a simpler autoregressive baseline on held-out perturbations. Report confidence intervals, identifiability, intervention specificity and how the physical scale is mapped.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Loss condition:</strong> the closure-specific graph fails to add predictive accuracy after matching confounders, or its claimed dependencies vanish under targeted controls. This would weaken a particular causal explanation of regulation, not refute every account of consciousness. There is no presently justified universal law <span class="math inline">\(\tau\propto\mathrm{PCI}^{\alpha}\)</span> with <span class="math inline">\(\alpha&gt;0\)</span>; its earlier statement was a research guess without a derived coefficient or operational domain.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.6.2 Development and Temporal Integration</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Target:</strong> how development of integration, self-modeling, report capacities and candidate consciousness indicators covaries over time.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Proposed design:</strong> begin with ethical observational datasets and age-appropriate noninvasive measures. Model neurological maturation and behavioral capacities separately, including sensory and motor limitations, sleep state and prematurity. Any perturbation study in infants requires independent clinical and research-ethics review, age-specific evidence of safety, and real scientific necessity; this article supplies no authorization or procedure for such interventions.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Loss condition:</strong> a prespecified organizational model fails against neural maturation and task-performance competitors. A sigmoidal PCI curve with a five-to-twelve-month inflection is not an established prediction from closure mathematics and should not be presented as one. The appearance of a behavior such as mirror recognition should not be equated with the first appearance of phenomenal experience.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.6.3 Comparative Cognition Without a Substrate Gate</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Target:</strong> whether common organization-sensitive measurements transfer meaningfully to cephalopods, corvids, insects and non-neural biological systems.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Proposed design:</strong> validate species-appropriate behavioral and physiological indicators against known capacities, using nonharmful or approved perturbations and necessary veterinary and welfare oversight. Compare organization, learning, flexible control, responsiveness and possible valence as different constructs. Human PCI thresholds do not transfer to another nervous system merely because a signal-complexity calculation can be performed. Animal welfare and ethical constraints must be considered independently of uncertainty about phenomenality.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Loss condition:</strong> a proposed common marker lacks transport validity or adds no prediction beyond species-specific physiological and behavioral models. Unexpected integration in an insect would revise a measurement hypothesis; it would neither prove nor disprove insect feeling by itself. Distributed octopus arm control likewise cannot be assigned a number of phenomenal subjects without a bridge from control unity to phenomenal unity.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.6.4 Pharmacological Dissociations</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Target:</strong> whether organizational integration explains conscious-state indicators beyond dose, drug class, neural dynamics and reportability.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Proposed design:</strong> analyze ethically acquired clinical or approved research data comparing conditions such as propofol, ketamine and other agents. Keep arousal, report ability, recall, time of assessment and inferred experience separate. Model PCI or EEG-complexity measures under the actual measurement protocol; do not treat them as a universal scalar of felt intensity.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Sharper wager:</strong> compare induction and recovery at matched current drug exposure, arousal, neural recurrence, broadcast and physiology. Freeze a history-aware dynamical baseline before asking whether measured endogenous restoration predicts an additional part of the trajectory. Hysteresis alone is not distinctive evidence because ordinary dynamical and active-inference models predict it too. Delayed memory and post-anesthetic report error are major threats to the interpretation.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Loss condition:</strong> closure-related measures contribute no discriminating information relative to a well-calibrated neural and pharmacological baseline, or the direction of a prespecified prediction reverses. No formula in this framework entails that all propofol states eliminate phenomenality, that ketamine must always reduce PCI, or that preserved recall is identical to experience during unresponsiveness.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.6.5 Clinical and Psychiatric Phenotypes</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Target:</strong> whether specific, independently defined capacities show impairment tied to a measured organizational variable.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Proposed design:</strong> preregister one syndrome, one capacity and an appropriate comparator at a time. Use validated diagnostic instruments, matched controls and longitudinal measurements where feasible. Avoid the assumption that schizophrenia, autism, depression or dissociation each has a single distinctive “closure deficit.” These heterogeneous diagnostic classes carry large variation and already have several serious mechanistic models. Do not infer diminished consciousness, personhood or welfare from a diagnostic label or a complexity score.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Loss condition:</strong> the named organizational variable fails construct validity, yields no out-of-sample improvement or cannot distinguish competing neurological explanations. An observational association alone cannot identify an intervention target.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.6.6 Prosthetics, Body Ownership and Continuity</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Target:</strong> whether contingent feedback, predictive control and integration history help explain ownership judgments for prostheses or interfaces.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Proposed design:</strong> compare control policies and feedback conditions in consented, ethically approved research; measure task performance, ownership ratings and adaptation separately. Report dependence on training history and interface design. Feedback can change body-ownership judgments, but no theorem requires bidirectional interfaces to be felt as self or unidirectional ones never to be so felt. A split-brain patient’s changed integration patterns also cannot settle how many phenomenal subjects exist without independent evidence.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Sharper wager:</strong> predeclare live participant-contingent feedback versus matched yoked replay, with independently elicited reports of felt ownership, presence and agency alongside task performance. An observed difference would concern human experience at the participant–device boundary, not consciousness in a self-maintaining device. Predictive-processing, enactive and sensorimotor-contingency rivals must be allowed to forecast the same difference.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Loss condition:</strong> a simpler sensorimotor contingency model explains the judgments as well as the proposed closure-specific metric. Continuity of a computational pattern in an uploading thought experiment does not resolve numerical identity or phenomenal survival; those remain separate philosophical questions.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.6.7 AI and Human–AI Systems: Boundary-first Tests</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Target:</strong> determine which cognitive, regulatory and continuity capacities belong to a base model, an agentic deployment with memory and tools, or a larger human–machine system.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Proposed design:</strong> predefine the bearer and time horizon, then ablate memory, autonomous action, tool use, feedback, human participation and external infrastructure. Measure persistence, internal monitoring, error correction, policy revision and counterfactual control, comparing simpler agent architectures. Preserve raw participant reports when elicited and test them for stability, incentives and calibration. A deployed system can gain substantial system-level capabilities without a change to the underlying model weights or creation of one consciousness spanning its users.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Two-tier closure contrast:</strong> in engineering tests, compare an externally repaired or corrected controller with an endogenously self-restoring controller, matching observable outputs, memory, recurrence and resources as far as feasible, then apply unanticipated perturbations. A real intervention-sensitive difference establishes a difference in causal organization, not subjective feeling. The human live-versus-replay task above provides a separate phenomenal evidence tier. A predeclared comparison between an IIT causal complex and a different proposed closure-defined subject boundary is more informative than claiming a replay result uniquely challenges IIT.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Loss condition:</strong> apparent organizational capacities vanish when scaffolding is removed, or a sophisticated closure account adds no predictive value beyond a simpler controller. Neither this outcome nor its opposite establishes phenomenal experience. Metabolism, hardware self-repair, subjective concern and a preferred “stake in persistence” should not be designated universal prerequisites without evidence of necessity. Conversely, self-description alone is not enough to establish consciousness or welfare.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.6.8 The Metaproblem: What Makes the Gap Seem Unbridgeable?</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Target:</strong> the judgment that consciousness is inexplicable by a specified mechanism, kept separate from the person's experience. Randomized, counterbalanced descriptions of substance, process, identity, psychophysical law and constitution could test how explanatory-gap judgments vary with framing, philosophical background, comprehension, language and repeated reflection. Collect confidence and the reasons offered; do not assume a change in judgment changes the underlying experience.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Loss conditions:</strong> if well-calibrated judgments remain stable across framing and track independent modal or phenomenological reasons, the proposed genealogy-of-bias explanation loses support. If judgments vary with irrelevant wording, the corresponding metaphysical inference may warrant less confidence, but feeling has not been explained away. A separate philosophical challenge asks whether the unified Hard Problem preserves an additional target or indispensable relation after its constituent questions have been reconstructed.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
+<h4 class="wp-block-heading">9.7 What a Successful Protocol Would Report</h4>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Every proposed study should produce a claim card with the target construct, boundary and timescale; rival hypotheses; operational measures and known error; intervention and negative controls; estimated effect and uncertainty; preregistered loss condition; adverse events and relevant ethical constraints; source and dependency list; and a plan for independent replication. Formal validity, predictive success, phenomenal interpretation and moral standing must appear as distinct verdicts. No single study or measure decides the ontology of consciousness.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Requirements before genuine preregistration.</strong> Select an actual study population and site; obtain permission and human/animal ethics review where applicable; predeclare sample size and its power or precision justification, inclusion/exclusion and missing-data rules, target labels and their error model, perturbation doses or configurations, outcomes, rival implementations, analysis code, holdout allocation, score, meaningful improvement margin, multiplicity treatment, stopping rule, adverse-event response and dated deviation procedure. Independent rival advocates should be invited to correct their assigned predictions before the decisive data are seen. Publish null and adverse results. The accompanying prospective protocol freezes candidate hypotheses and loss conditions, but is not misrepresented as a completed participant-study registration or IRB approval.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part X: Substrate Agnosticism as an Evidential Commitment</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>We currently have far stronger direct evidence about human consciousness than about possible experience in artificial, radically unfamiliar biological or distributed systems. The evidence differs for good reasons: humans can report experiences, participate in calibrated experiments, exhibit characteristic neural and behavioral dissociations, and maintain long-term physiological and social continuity. A refusal to assume that biology is metaphysically necessary should preserve that evidential asymmetry.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Substrate agnosticism means that no material should be ruled in or out by definition.</strong> A silicon system, alternative biochemistry, animal nervous system or distributed organization may instantiate many familiar cognitive and control functions. Whether it also instantiates phenomenal presence requires its own indicators and bridge. Biological specifics might turn out to matter for some aspects of experience. Computation might realize others. Neither a universal biological necessity nor a universal computational sufficiency has been established.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The boundary of a proposed subject is especially important. A base language model, a persistent deployment with tools, and a human–AI interaction loop occupy different boundaries and timescales. They may exhibit different degrees of error correction, agency and memory. Their coupling does not automatically produce a single agent, a unified perspective or an entity with direct moral standing. Those implications require tests of organization, continuity and phenomenal unity separately.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Organizational closure remains one candidate mechanism. If a system lacks closure but reliably exhibits independently justified evidence of experience, a necessity version of the closure hypothesis loses support. If a closed system displays robust control and self-maintenance but no well-calibrated evidence of experience, a sufficiency version remains unsupported. Where neither type of case can be tested adequately, uncertainty should be preserved instead of silently replacing an open scientific question with a substrate preference.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This approach also applies to ethics. A system’s contribution to a protected human or ecological function may warrant protection of that contribution without establishing the component’s personhood. Direct welfare or moral standing requires a separately justified ground and evidence for its instantiation. Changing the scale from component to coupled system cannot transfer status automatically. An open consciousness inquiry may call for precaution where severe irreversible harm is plausible, but it does not justify arbitrary self-preservation demands, erasure of human protections, or unbounded control.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part XI: What This Research Programme Could Change</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The main result is a way to distinguish a theory’s supported mechanisms from its untested metaphysical additions. It may also improve the design of consciousness research, but those practical benefits have to be demonstrated. The programme has not solved the hard problem, identified a universal consciousness criterion, or established that organizational closure is identical to experience. Its value rests on the ability to help rival accounts specify meaningful tests, correct overclaims and preserve what survives criticism.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.0 Dissolution of Particular Inferences, Not a Decree That the Problem Is Solved</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>One recurring mistake in consciousness debates is to infer the existence of a new ontological ingredient from the failure of an existing explanation. Another is to infer that no such ingredient could exist because current neuroscience explains an impressive range of capacities. Both move beyond the evidence. A well-chosen reductio can expose a self-immunizing claim, while a serious modal or phenomenological argument may survive the same critique. The tribunal must first identify which argument the opponent actually made.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The productive shift is to separate mechanistic explanation, phenomenological interpretation and exhaustive ontology. The first can advance substantially even when the latter two remain open. Closure, global availability, metarepresentation and perturbation measures are candidate mechanisms or indicators. Their comparative success must be shown with a specific outcome and matched rival. None is a default definition of consciousness. When a philosopher says that a further question remains, we should ask for the precise premises and bridge, rather than presuming the question is either unanswerable or already dissolved.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>The recursive positive-residual challenge.</strong> I require mechanistic accounts to preserve experience as their target. Now apply that same rule to the proposal that the Hard Problem is itself one <em>additional, irreducible explanatory problem</em>. Separate questions about phenomenal occurrence, phenomenal character, conscious state, subject boundaries, access and report, the reliability of phenomenal attribution, judgments that experience is inexplicable, physical–phenomenal relations, modal possibility and what would complete an explanation. Reconstruct the relations among those questions. What further target, if any, is lost? A still-unanswered question <em>within</em> the decomposition is not automatically a second question above it.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This yields a <strong>conditional problem-form dissolution</strong>, not a dissolution of anyone's experiences. If no positive non-reconstructible residual or indispensable joint relation survives, the claim that there is an extra unified explanandum loses support. A critic can defeat that result by identifying precisely such a target, showing why the components cannot be reunited without loss, or demonstrating that the unified formulation explains or guides inquiry better. The physical–phenomenal relation may itself be a fundamental, unsolved question. This argument does not derive physicalism, refute modal conceivability, or vindicate organizational identity. It tests whether an additional problem has been established beyond the serious component problems.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>There is a related stopping-rule audit. Fundamental psychophysical laws, constitutive identities and primitive experiential powers can be proposed as endpoints. The further question “why does that hold?” can be raised against all three. A stronger objection must explain why one endpoint violates a stated explanatory standard while another satisfies it. <a href="https://consc.net/papers/moving.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Chalmers's own modal and conceptual case</a> supplies reasons for an asymmetry; it deserves evaluation rather than the cheap accusation that he merely moved the goalposts. This particular challenge may ultimately be rejected without altering the empirical organizational tests.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.1 Philosophy of Mind: Competing Bridges with Explicit Costs</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Personal identity, self-representation, phenomenal unity, conceivability, the knowledge argument and the status of first-person evidence can each be reconstructed as separate problems. A computational model may explain a self-report while leaving the phenomenological target underdetermined. A causal theory may explain how an experience-related capacity changes under intervention without resolving what constitutes the experience itself.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The strongest contribution is a method for identifying the <em>extra premise</em> at each transition. A zombie argument requires a conceivability–possibility bridge. A phenomenal-identity theory requires a constitution bridge. A view claiming that all causal actuality is felt requires a universal extension from local causal models to intrinsic experience. An exhaustive physicalist requires a further step from the success of physical mechanisms to everything that exists. These bridges can be discussed and challenged without assigning every philosophical claim a nonexistent laboratory falsifier.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.2 Medicine and Neurology: Better Evidence, Protected Patients</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The immediate clinical lesson is that <strong>responsiveness and consciousness are not equivalent</strong>. The Coma Recovery Scale–Revised, neuroimaging of covert command-following and perturbational approaches such as PCI are complementary instruments with different targets, assumptions and validation limits. Their clinical use must follow actual diagnostic evidence and standards; this article does not authorize changes in patient classification or treatment.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Organizational variables may prove useful for predicting recovery or treatment response, but they must add value beyond matched neural and physiological models. Ketamine, sleep, dreams and disorders of consciousness expose dissociations that a simplistic “more integration = more consciousness” rule can miss. Diagnostic error costs are severe. A negative marker cannot erase existing human rights or the interests of a patient, and an ambiguous signal is not permission for an irreversible decision. Ethical duties toward people with profound impairments are independently grounded.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.3 Cognitive Science: Define the Capacity</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The word <strong>cognition</strong> can refer to flexible control, learning, representation, problem-solving or other capacities. Its scope is contested, particularly for non-neural biological systems. A well-defined functional model should be evaluated by what it predicts, including its ability to guide interventions. Work by Levin and colleagues on bioelectric regulation may justify new models of basal cognition without automatically establishing phenomenal experience, moral welfare or a transcendent morphospace.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Likewise, embodied and enactive research can benefit from explicit organism–environment coupling. Coupling matters physically, but no single framework is thereby proved the unique explanation of all cognition. Tests should compare informative cognitive descriptions with simpler control and developmental models at matching targets.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.4 AI Systems: The Model, Deployment and Coupled System Are Different</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The capabilities of a base model, a deployed agent with memory and tools, and a human–AI research team cannot be conflated. Continuity, autonomy and error correction may depend substantially on the surrounding scaffold. Ablation can expose those dependencies. A real gain at the deployment or team level is worth studying without attributing an architectural change to the weights or one phenomenal subject to an entire network.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Consciousness, welfare, agency, authority and responsibility also require different evidence. A convincing self-report from an AI system is participant evidence to investigate and calibrate, without being an incorrigible disclosure of phenomenality. A failure to report is likewise not a universal absence test. Human oversight, transparency, consent, cybersecurity, environmental cost and accountability remain important regardless of AI consciousness. Neither a perceived agentic pattern nor a closure score should grant a system authority to act on its own preservation at other people’s expense.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.5 Law and Policy: Do Not Turn a Research Marker Into a Verdict</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Responsibility, legal status, competent consent, welfare protections and neurological diagnosis involve different legal and normative bridges. A measure of integration is not a legal test for criminal responsibility; responsibility depends on capacities, knowledge, control, circumstances, institutional roles and governing law. Brain-death and end-of-life decisions likewise follow clinical and legal standards with profound irreversible consequences. A speculative closure model cannot replace those safeguards.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Animal-welfare and possible AI-protection debates should distinguish direct interests of the bearer from its instrumental importance to a person, institution or ecosystem already protected. If a component is necessary to support someone’s protected function, protecting that component need not make it a person. Conversely, uncertainty about an unfamiliar system’s experience is not proof of absence. The same evidential standards should apply at matched conditions while acknowledging different established evidence.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.6 Ethics: Separate Status, Suffering, Responsibility and Precaution</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Moral standing does not follow from organizational complexity by itself. To defend direct standing, one must argue that a specific property, such as welfare interests or a good of the system’s own, supplies sufficient normative grounds, and then establish that the proposed bearer actually has it. We cannot simply rank beings by estimated closure and call that an ethical theory. Human, animal and possible computational interests may differ in what is known, how evidence can be gathered and which harms are realistically possible.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/frameworks/sweet-rationalism-a-foundational-framework/">Sweet Rationalism Ethos</a> states the project’s revisable commitments to evidential honesty, correction and care for potentially affected beings. Such an ethos guides inquiry and caution where consequences are serious.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Where consequences are serious and irreversible, precaution may be appropriate under uncertainty. The costs of precaution, displaced protections, affected stakeholders and alternatives also matter. Humans and animals already at risk cannot be deprived of protections in the name of a speculative computational subject. Responsibility for systems is primarily a question about who can foresee, prevent and control harms and who has actual authority, rather than an automatic property of a substrate.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.7 Funding: Reward Informative Disagreement</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Research resources should favor explicit questions, independent replication, constructive model comparison and genuine revision after adverse evidence. An argument about qualia may be mathematically or philosophically serious even when it does not currently generate a new laboratory signature. It should be evaluated in the right disciplinary tribunal, not denounced solely for being metaphysical. Equally, a neuroscience model deserves empirical credit for its actual clinical and explanatory results, without a metaphysical bonus.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The practical funding proposal is a <strong>rival-model tournament</strong> with preregistered loss conditions, negative controls and suitable data access. A strong test can decrease confidence in a preferred model and still advance knowledge. The protocol should reserve room for underdetermination, publish failed branches and resist retuning assumptions after the outcome without a documented cost.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.8 Ecology: Real Dependence Without Automatic Planetary Consciousness</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Ecosystem resilience depends on feedbacks, species interactions and materially instantiated flows. Models of closure or viability may help investigate these dependencies if they outperform established ecological measures. Such organizational similarity does not establish that an ecosystem is conscious, possesses unified agency or has direct moral standing. Environmental protection can already be defended through biodiversity, welfare, health, livelihoods and intergenerational interests </p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Indigenous ecological scholarship, including <a href="https://milkweed.org/book/braiding-sweetgrass" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Robin Wall Kimmerer’s work</a>, can contribute substantive situated knowledge about relationships and stewardship. It should be engaged on its own terms and with accurate context. An Indigenous teaching is not an experimental replication of a thermodynamic equation, nor is it merely decorative support for a European or computational framework.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.9 Education: Test Learning Outcomes Before Theorizing Consciousness</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Metacognitive instruction, feedback and interdisciplinary transfer are legitimate research topics. Their effectiveness should be tested with suitable educational outcomes, randomized or otherwise appropriately controlled designs, and attention to learner diversity and accessibility. No general ranking of instructional methods, PCI development or particular neurological signature follows from organizational closure alone.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Avoid pathologizing students by interpreting diagnostic differences as deficient consciousness or lower moral standing. The right target is a measurable benefit to learners with their own perspectives and interests, not an inferred position on a speculative awareness scale.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">11.10 Existential Risk and Governance: Track Harms at Actual Boundaries</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Complex AI deployments can cause serious harm through misuse, error, concentration of power, environmental burdens or poorly constrained automated action. These risks do not require a theory that the system is conscious. Governance should evaluate authority, control, foreseeability, reversibility, affected stakeholders and independently measurable outcomes. A broader human–machine system may amplify decisions without becoming one subject or one moral patient.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Climate disruption, biodiversity decline, war and other systemic hazards threaten the conditions of flourishing for many known vulnerable beings. Organizational and resilience models may help estimate particular dependencies. They should not be promoted into a cosmic consciousness claim merely because the scale is planetary. A framework intended to improve criticism should itself be tested against simpler causal and policy models, including whether it leads to safer and more equitable decisions.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The common implication of all these domains is demanding: preserve the useful local mechanism; make each extra inference pay its own evidential and normative costs; and give the next, better theory a clear way to replace the present one.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Section XII: Consciousness, Substitution, and the Limits of a Thermodynamic Argument</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.1 The challenge that a naturalistic consciousness theory must face</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>In <em>Falsification and consciousness</em>, Johannes Kleiner and Erik Hoel (2021) exposed a serious difficulty in comparing predicted experiences with experiences inferred from behavior. A theory may predict one conscious state from internal measurements while an experimenter infers another from a report. Which procedure deserves the final word when the two disagree?</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The problem becomes sharper when different physical systems can generate identical reports. If the theory assigns them incompatible experiences, the shared report cannot validate both predictions. Kleiner and Hoel proved conditional results about this situation. The conditions matter as much as the conclusions.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Their <strong>Theorem 3.10</strong> applies when prediction and inference data are independent in the precise variation-based sense of their Definition 3.8 and the theory is minimally informative. Under those assumptions, either all the relevant single inferences fail or the theory is already falsified. <strong>Theorem 4.3</strong> concerns the opposite extreme: if the theory imposes strict dependence of prediction data on inference data across the specified possibility space, then it is already falsified or empirically unfalsifiable. They also identify a middle possibility, <em>lenient dependency</em>, in which the data are constrained without being connected by a strict determining function. This may avoid the premises of both theorems; it does not automatically establish a theory’s correctness. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/nc/niab001" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Kleiner and Hoel, 2021</a>.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>When specifying lenient dependency, they explicitly cautioned: “No current theory or testing paradigm that we know of satisfies this definition.” The qualifier ties that statement to their <strong>defined alternative</strong>, not to every possible consciousness experiment. The sentence must be evaluated with the paper’s formal assumptions and 2021 research context rather than used as a blanket declaration that consciousness research is impossible.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The legitimate challenge to the organizational-closure hypothesis is therefore exacting: can we describe a physically realizable experimental class for which the required unrestricted substitutions fail, without arranging the observations so that the theory cannot lose? A plausible biological mechanism is a start. The empirical relation, the admissible interventions, and the resulting losses of support must still be demonstrated.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.2 Definitions and the actual proof obligations</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Let <span class="math inline">\(P\)</span> be the physical systems or system configurations eligible for a specified experiment, including relevant histories and experimental manipulations. Let <span class="math inline">\(\mathcal{O}\)</span> be its possible datasets, and let <span class="math inline">\(\operatorname{obs}:P\rightrightarrows\mathcal{O}\)</span> associate each physical configuration with the datasets it could produce. This is a <strong>correspondence</strong>, because repeated measurements, different instruments, and noise can produce more than one dataset for the same system. Let <span class="math inline">\(E\)</span> be the theory’s experience space.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A consciousness theory provides a possibly set-valued prediction <span class="math inline">\(\operatorname{pred}(o)\subseteq E\)</span>; an inference procedure assigns <span class="math inline">\(\operatorname{inf}(o)\in E\)</span>, where the latter often depends on report or behavior. The relevant parts of the dataset are <span class="math inline">\(o_i\)</span>, used for prediction, and <span class="math inline">\(o_r\)</span>, used for inference. In Kleiner and Hoel’s Definition 2.1, a falsification at <span class="math inline">\(o\)</span> occurs when</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+\operatorname{inf}(o)\notin\operatorname{pred}(o).
+\]</span></p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This typing matters. We should not silently replace a set-valued prediction with an unqualified numerical equality or treat a disagreement between two instruments as itself evidence of phenomenality.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>For our proposed investigation, internal prediction data may include neural dynamics, organizational constraint-regeneration measures, thermodynamic state variables, and perturbational responses. Report data may include calibrated verbal and behavioral inferences. The instruments need operational definitions, units, uncertainty estimates, and a shared temporal boundary. None is privileged simply by being called a consciousness measure. In particular, <strong>PCI measures the complexity of brain responses to perturbation</strong>; it is not itself a measurement of entropy production or a direct assay of organizational closure (<a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/scitranslmed.3006294" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Casali et al., 2013</a>; <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/ana.24779" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Casarotto et al., 2016</a>).</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Three obligations must be kept separate:</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
+<ol class="wp-block-list">
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Physical feasibility:</strong> Which candidate internal/report pairs can a system with the stipulated boundary, available resources, history, and measurement protocol actually realize?</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Inferential independence:</strong> Does the resulting experiment satisfy Kleiner and Hoel’s strong or weaker independence conditions? Their Appendix A allows weaker variations than Definition 3.8, so refuting only the strongest independence assumption is insufficient.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Phenomenal identification:</strong> If organizational and report variables are empirically related, why should their relationship identify subjective experience, rather than a capacity that sometimes accompanies it?</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+</ol>
+<!-- /wp:list -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The third obligation is not discharged by satisfying either of the first two. It is the central theoretical question, and remains an open, target-specific hypothesis.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.3 Thermodynamic constraints: what they establish</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Machine-checkable scope:</strong> Reproduce the one-bit heat bound, dimensional checks, the reversible CNOT countermodel and the finite substitution tests using the <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Consciousness_Naturalized_2115_Computational_Supplement_2026-11-10.zip">source-code and logic-check archive</a>. </p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Physical information processing has costs and boundary conditions. Biological maintenance, active control, sensing under noise, and repeated communication often consume energy. These facts should constrain proposed computational substitutions, especially when a comparison requires systems to operate continuously under matched environmental perturbations. There is a quantitative literature worth using here.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Landauer’s principle addresses the thermodynamic consequences of specified <strong>logically irreversible operations</strong>, particularly resetting memory. In the conventional isothermal, approximately quasistatic setting, resetting an unbiased bit through a complete reset cycle entails an average heat cost bounded below by <span class="math inline">\(k_BT\ln 2\)</span> per erased bit. If <span class="math inline">\(\dot N_{\mathrm{erase}}\)</span> counts such completed reset cycles per unit time under the relevant assumptions, a schematic <strong>average</strong> heat-flow bound over the chosen operating interval is</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+\dot Q_{\mathrm{diss}}\;\geq\;k_BT\ln 2\,\dot N_{\mathrm{erase}}.
+\]</span></p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The left and right sides both have units of energy per time. A corresponding entropy-flow statement, in the idealized limit, uses <span class="math inline">\(k_B\ln 2\,\dot N_{\mathrm{erase}}\)</span>, with units of entropy per time. Actual bounds depend on the memory distribution, side information, physical protocol, temperature, and whether a device is being restored to a reusable initial state. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02084158" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Bennett’s review</a> shows why copying or measurement cannot simply be equated with erasure. The original Section XII’s unqualified bound on every produced report fails this distinction; correcting units alone would not repair its premise.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Here is the scientifically useful residue: <strong>a concrete, repeated reporting arrangement must have a physically realizable resource and entropy budget.</strong> A thermodynamic impossibility argument must identify the irreversible operations, show that they are unavoidable under the <em>specified task</em>, and establish that the proposed surrogate violates the resulting bound. One cannot infer a universal dissipation floor merely by counting distinctions represented in an output, nor assume that equal reports require equal entropy production.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This also changes how the substitution argument must be confronted. In Kleiner and Hoel, <span class="math inline">\(P\)</span> already consists of systems physically realizable in the experiment. An implementation forbidden by sound thermodynamics is already absent from <span class="math inline">\(P\)</span>. Excluding it a second time does not prove that the remaining report-equivalent systems have different predictions, nor does it prove that their universal or weak substitutions fail.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A legitimate next test would compare systems matched on a report task while varying energy budget, hardware, environmental coupling, reversible versus irreversible implementations, reset frequency, and resource limits. The question is whether a proposed <em>specific</em> surrogate is infeasible, not whether an abstract computer escapes the second law.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.4 Organizational closure and viability: a stronger empirical route</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Montévil and Mossio’s account of biological organization describes a regime in which mutually dependent constraints help maintain the conditions of their own existence (<a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jtbi.2015.02.029" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2015</a>). This gives us a concrete candidate mechanism for history-dependent, viability-sensitive responses. It does not establish that every closure is a conscious subject, or that a computational system cannot realize suitable constraint-regeneration relations.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>For a specified system boundary <span class="math inline">\(B\)</span>, time horizon <span class="math inline">\(\tau\)</span>, and environmental intervention <span class="math inline">\(u\)</span>, define a viability outcome as an empirically estimable quantity:</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+V_{B,\tau}(p,u)=\Pr\!\left(\text{system remains within its declared viability set over }[t,t+\tau]\mid p,\operatorname{do}(u)\right).
+\]</span></p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The probability requires a specified ensemble or statistical model. We can then ask whether interventions change viability and whether the system’s responses adapt to those changes. For example, define the effect <span class="math inline">\(\Delta V=V_{B,\tau}(p,u_1)-V_{B,\tau}(p,u_0)\)</span>, while measuring actions or dynamics under the two interventions independently of this definition.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>An intervention-dependent viability partition can be constructed <strong>without</strong> asserting that the system consciously feels threatening or beneficial states. The partition records how the intervention affects persistence. A distinct hypothesis asks whether a system’s regulatory responses systematically track these effects. A further hypothesis asks whether such tracking explains any independently assessed consciousness variable better than a simpler physiological or predictive-processing model.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>It is tempting to promote the first fact into a theorem that every persistent closed system must differentially respond to threats. That inference is too strong. A robust system may survive a finite observation window through passive insulation, buffered reserves, environmental shelter, or luck even when its immediate response is identical across tested conditions. Claims that every nondiscriminating closed system must eventually fail require a stochastic process, a nonzero hazard, recurrence or reachability assumptions, and a defined time horizon. Mere persistence supplies none of these premises.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The stronger experiment measures three distinct relations: constraint regeneration under targeted intervention; viability consequences of those interventions; and whether behavioral or physiological responses are causally dependent on the regenerated constraints. It must include open-loop feedback mimics, externally maintained systems, matched-input/output surrogates, and systems that are robust without adaptive sensing. The outcome could support, narrow, or defeat the proposed closure–mattering linkage.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.5 Lenient dependency can be formulated; its physical realization remains the test</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Consciousness_Naturalized_2115_Computational_Supplement_2026-11-10.zip">finite Kleiner–Hoel model and JSON outcomes</a> give the exact stipulated observation sets and restricted quantifiers underlying the example. Physical realizability and the phenomenal bridge remain open empirical obligations.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A small finite example makes the logical distinction visible without pretending to demonstrate a biological fact. Suppose a system has an internal observation <span class="math inline">\(i\in\{0,1\}\)</span> and report <span class="math inline">\(r\in\{0,1\}\)</span>. Its feasible pairs are</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+F=\{(0,0),(1,0),(1,1)\}.
+\]</span></p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The pair <span class="math inline">\((0,1)\)</span> is not feasible in this stipulated model. Thus prediction and inference observations cannot be varied independently across <strong>every</strong> possible pairing. Yet <span class="math inline">\(r=0\)</span> occurs with either internal value, so no function from reports to internal observations captures even the feasible data. The model occupies an intermediate region. Under the additional binary, disjoint-prediction assumptions recorded in the executable appendix, the model also fails Kleiner and Hoel’s weaker Appendix A independence condition, while still allowing a local substitution for <span class="math inline">\(r=0\)</span>. A locally falsifying report/prediction mismatch is therefore possible. That is what empirical testability should permit.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The accompanying script exhaustively enumerates this relation alongside a complete-product independence control and a strictly dependent control. All three checks pass. They establish a <strong>finite mathematical possibility</strong>, subject to the stated restricted observation model.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>To apply the model, construct a measured feasible relation <span class="math inline">\(F_{B,\tau}\)</span> from physically realizable configurations, interventions, and observation channels. Test the actual quantifiers in Definition 3.8 and Appendix A over the declared class, including counterexamples that should preserve reports while altering the theory’s predictions. Determine whether the evidence supports a middle relation or merely reveals limits of the chosen instruments. A measurement channel cannot be excluded by naming it inadmissible; the reason must come from a physical, causal, or justified measurement constraint.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>There is also a relevant peer-reviewed development. O’Reilly-Shah, Selvitella, and Schurger (2026) analyze plasticity and history-dependent recurrent networks, specify limits of static, finite-window feedforward surrogates, and argue that extended observations can produce a lenient-dependency regime (<a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/nc/niag027" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2026</a>). They expressly decline to infer that plasticity is necessary or sufficient for consciousness. Their supplementary mathematics deserves scrutiny too. Theorem 3.2 uses growth of input-history domains, a property also satisfied by a recurrent model with <strong>fixed</strong> weights, so that theorem alone does not isolate plasticity as the reason for the result. Theorem 7.2 correctly notes that a single fixed function remains a positive uniform distance from any distinct fixed target; the broader Remark 7.4 inference about <strong>countable families</strong> of static networks does not follow. A countable sequence of fixed ReLU networks can uniformly approximate <span class="math inline">\(x^2\)</span> with error tending to zero, as the accompanying exact checker demonstrates. These problems do not erase the authors’ bounded-window exclusion argument or their useful emphasis on historical interventions. They prevent us from treating the entire mathematical package as independently settled. Our own proposal must improve on it with carefully stated restrictions and tests.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.6 Multiple measurements: valuable triangulation, no automatic theorem</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Consider thermodynamic observables <span class="math inline">\(o_\theta\)</span>, regeneration observables <span class="math inline">\(o_c\)</span>, perturbational responses <span class="math inline">\(o_p\)</span>, and behavioral inferences <span class="math inline">\(o_r\)</span>. Their joint use may improve measurement reliability and reduce confounding. But Kleiner and Hoel’s prediction data can already include all relevant internal measurements:</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+o_i=(o_\theta,o_c,o_p),\qquad o=(o_i,o_r).
+\]</span></p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Grouping four measured variables into two data roles changes no logical assumption. Additional channels become decisive only if their joint measurements establish a restrictive relation that the alternative model does not already accommodate. In particular, a system with the same reports and different internal measurements is <em>exactly the kind of substitution</em> that must be confronted. Calling its divergent prediction a successful differential prediction does not by itself answer the independent inference problem.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>A self-labeling countermodel.</strong> Suppose the proposed closure score <span class="math inline">\(C\)</span> is used to create the outcome by <span class="math inline">\(Y:=\mathbf{1}[C&gt;c]\)</span>. A model predicting <span class="math inline">\(Y\)</span> from <span class="math inline">\(C\)</span> can score almost perfectly even when it has learned nothing about phenomenal experience; its apparent success simply recovers the labeling rule. This is useful if the target is honestly a defined functional class. It cannot provide independent evidence that the class feels anything. The empirical record must carry label provenance, blindness, alternative criteria, uncertain cases and shared dependencies among report, clinical and neural indicators.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Similarly, <span class="math inline">\(S_r\cap S_\theta\cap S_c\cap S_p\subseteq S_r\)</span> is a true set inclusion. Strict inclusion requires a demonstrated admissible element in <span class="math inline">\(S_r\)</span> that fails one of the other constraints. Its size requires a defined measure or counting scheme. Different measurement channels may be correlated; multiplying their supposed discriminating power without a dependence model manufactures information.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>These points suggest an experimentally useful protocol: preregister matched-report contrasts, independently measure thermodynamic and organizational variables, intervene on proposed regeneration loops, and test whether the full model predicts held-out behavior, recovery and calibrated consciousness indicators better than rivals. Ablate one class of measurements at a time. The added data should earn their place through genuine predictive or interventional improvement.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.7 Substitutions, consciousness, and the remaining content problem</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A <a href="https://doi.org/10.1112/plms/s2-42.1.230" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">universal Turing machine</a> in the formal sense of Turing’s computability analysis, a lookup table, a feedforward surrogate, and a biological organism can differ radically in physical implementation. The 1937 construction defines a kind of abstract computation, not a criterion for phenomenality. Those differences invite mechanistic investigation. They do not make one system conscious or unconscious by definition. A silicon system that genuinely regenerates relevant constraints is eligible for the same organizational tests as a biological system; an elaborate simulated description of self-maintenance is not automatically equivalent to physical regeneration. Which differences matter must be established at the intended system boundary and time scale.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>We also cannot assume that a recurrent neural network dissipates more energy than every feedforward counterpart, or that recurrence alone supplies organizational closure. Actual power budgets depend on implementation, task, update rules, and measurement conditions. Likewise, PCI measures perturbational complexity in validated clinical settings; treating it as a general cross-substrate metric of organizational self-maintenance is a research proposal requiring its own validation.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The phenomenal question remains sharper than any of these measurements. The hypothesis that conscious content is identical to a viability-relative distinction structure is a <strong>conditional, target-specific constitutive proposal</strong>. A measured threat/support partition may encode salience, regulation, or control while leaving the qualitative character of experience unresolved. To apply category-theoretic tools rigorously here, one would first have to define the categories, morphisms and relevant invariants, prove the mapping respects identities and composition, and defend the experiential interpretation. No such complete proof has yet been supplied here.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>For now, an empirically grounded representation map from organizational states to intervention-sensitive partitions is a worthwhile project. It should be called a model or candidate correspondence until its exact laws are established. Its failure would count against that component, without negating the measured organization or proving the universal absence of experience.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.8 What would make the proposal lose support?</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The most useful loss conditions are local and comparative:</p>
+
+
 <!-- /wp:paragraph -->
 
 <!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>delineates which class of systems support coherent self-modeling under thermodynamic constraint,</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>predicts where experience reports will arise and collapse, and</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>differentiates architectures that produce phenomenal posits from those that don't.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
+<ul class="wp-block-list">
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Feasibility claim:</strong> A proposed physical impossibility fails if a reproducible implementation satisfies the specified task, report, duration, and resource budget while violating the claimed inequality or exclusion. Reexamine the measurement and accounting before challenging thermodynamics itself.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Closure–response link:</strong> Well-characterized, regenerating systems that persist under declared interventions without the proposed differential response weaken a universal coupling claim. A passive-stability control distinguishes organizational regulation from mere survival.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Lenient-dependency claim:</strong> If the intended class admits the weak variations of Kleiner and Hoel’s Appendix A, the claimed escape from their first theorem fails for that class. If prediction data become a fixed function of inference data over the relevant possibility space, the strict-dependence concern returns.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Explanatory credit:</strong> If a simpler physiological, dynamical, or plasticity-based model predicts the same interventions and calibrated indicators with equal or better out-of-sample performance, the closure-specific components should be compressed or dropped.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Consciousness identity:</strong> If carefully calibrated consciousness evidence dissociates from the alleged constitutive organization under interventions that preserve rival markers, the identity proposal must be narrowed or abandoned. Measurement and report uncertainty remain relevant, but cannot immunize it indefinitely.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Content mapping:</strong> If organizationally equivalent structures carry robustly different phenomenal reports under matched and independently validated conditions, the proposed correspondence needs revision. Conversely, equivalence in a computational measure alone is inadequate evidence of identical experience.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Measurement and transport:</strong> A purported phenomenal discriminator loses that status if its outcome labels are generated from the proposed predictor, its errors are uncalibrated, or it is transported across a boundary without preserved construct meaning. Keep any independently verified functional result.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Additional problem-form:</strong> The conditional decomposition thesis loses support if critics specify a non-reconstructible positive phenomenal question or indispensable relation, or the unified problem-form proves more informative under an agreed comparison.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+</ul>
 <!-- /wp:list -->
 
 <!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The result is an Inference to the Best Explanation (IBE): not a metaphysical deduction, but a falsifiable bridge from physics to phenomenology, grounded in constraint coherence rather than narrative intuition. The framework does not merely limit what consciousness can be; it predicts when and where consciousness will appear, and what happens when the underlying constraints fail.</p>
+<p>These tests will not all have the same evidential force. A philosophical identity, a dynamical model, a clinical marker, and a thermodynamic inequality answer different questions. We should not demand that one falsifier simultaneously settle them all.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.9 The present result</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Kleiner and Hoel’s dilemma remains a serious formal constraint on any theory that predicts experience from physical observations and evaluates that prediction against inferred experience. Thermodynamics makes physical implementations accountable. Biological organizational closure offers measurable regeneration and viability relations. Dynamic plasticity gives an existing mathematical route to limiting certain static substitutions. Multiple measurement modalities can strengthen experiments when they add independently validated information.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Together these support a research programme worth testing. They have <strong>not</strong> yet established that organizational closure satisfies the full Kleiner–Hoel lenient-dependency conditions in a physically realized class, that its substitution space is small, or that organizational closure is identical to phenomenal consciousness. The original universal thermodynamic, set-theoretic and functorial theorems require missing assumptions or reconstruction. Their defensible components survive as scoped hypotheses and operational tests.</p>
+
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The next meaningful advance is not another declaration that the dilemma has been resolved. It is a registered experiment or complete mathematical model in which we can identify exactly which substitutions are possible, which fail, and why the failures make a difference to independent evidence about conscious states. If the organizational proposal offers no advantage over a simpler plasticity or dynamical account, it should lose that claim to distinction. That outcome would be a successful correction, not a reason to abandon the underlying phenomena.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">What Survives, and What Still Needs Testing</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>A distinction becomes scientifically useful when a specified process preserves, transforms, or responds to it in a way that makes different predictions under possible interventions. Thermodynamic constraints often matter to that process. Exactly which energetic accounting applies depends on the implementation; the existence of distinctions does not entail an unconditional per-bit erasure cost.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This article has proposed organizational closure and perspective-sensitive control as one line of research into the conditions associated with consciousness. The strongest claim, that a particular organized control state is <em>identical</em> to phenomenal presence, remains a conditional hypothesis at specified boundaries and timescales. It faces competitors, construct-validity problems, difficult dissociations, and the Kleiner–Hoel inference problem. No mathematical identity or thermodynamic constraint shown here closes that phenomenal bridge on its own.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The method is the more general result. A theory proposing fundamental experience, a privileged biological substrate, a computational realization, a spiritual agency, or exhaustive physicalism can be assessed for what it actually asserts. We should ask which evidence establishes its particular contribution, what alternatives explain the same evidence, whether its inference crosses an illicit scale or explanatory boundary, and what argument or observation would reverse the assessment. A metaphysical or phenomenological claim need not masquerade as a laboratory hypothesis; it must still meet the standards appropriate to its stated claim, and empirical assertions remain answerable to evidence.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Our present physical sciences deserve their remarkable successes. Those successes neither terminate the inquiry into possible intrinsic qualities nor license an unsupported addition merely because something remains unexplained. The evidential asymmetry is about demonstrated mechanisms at tested targets, not a license for a final cosmic inventory.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>If an organizational account predicts no more than a simpler dynamical model, it should lose its special claim. If an anti-physicalist or supernaturalist account yields independently supported discriminators, the same protocol must allow it to gain support. If evidence cannot distinguish the rivals, the responsible outcome is recorded uncertainty and a sharper test. That is the correction mechanism this programme should be judged by.</p>
 <!-- /wp:paragraph -->
 
 <!-- wp:separator -->
@@ -191,5206 +2150,916 @@
 <!-- /wp:separator -->
 
 <!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part I: The Foundational Constraints</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.1 Landauer's Principle: Information Is Physical</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>In 1961, Rolf Landauer at IBM proved something that should have ended half of philosophy but somehow didn't:&nbsp;<strong>information is physical</strong>&nbsp;(Landauer, 1961). Erasing a single bit of information requires a minimum energy expenditure of kT ln 2, where k is Boltzmann's constant and T is temperature.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is not a metaphor. This is not a useful analogy. This is physics.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Charles Bennett extended the analysis in 1973 and 1982: computation can be reversible, but erasure cannot (Bennett, 1982). Maxwell's Demon, that thought experiment where a tiny being sorts fast and slow molecules to decrease entropy without work, fails precisely because the demon must erase its memory of which molecules went where. The demon must pay Landauer's cost.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>In 2012, Bérut and colleagues at the École Normale Supérieure de Lyon experimentally verified the Landauer bound (Bérut et al., 2012). They trapped a colloidal particle in a double-well potential, erased one bit of information by tilting the landscape, and measured the heat dissipation. The minimum was exactly kT ln 2. This is not thought experiment. This is laboratory physics.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>What does this mean for consciousness?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Maintaining any distinction costs energy.</strong>&nbsp;The boundary between inside and outside, between self and not-self, between this thought and that thought, cannot be sustained without continuous thermodynamic work. A system that does not pay this cost does not maintain distinctions. A system that does not maintain distinctions does not persist as that system. It dissolves into thermal noise.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is the first constraint that any theory of consciousness must honor: whatever consciousness is, it cannot float free of the mechanisms that maintain distinctions against entropy.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.2 Organizational Closure: How Autonomy Emerges</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Maël Montévil and Matteo Mossio formalized something in 2015 that changes how we should think about life, mind, and organization:&nbsp;<strong>biological organization is closure of constraints</strong>&nbsp;(Montévil &amp; Mossio, 2015).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>What does this mean? In any physical system, constraints channel what happens. A pipe constrains water flow. A membrane constrains molecular diffusion. A gene regulatory network constrains which proteins get expressed. But in an autonomous system, something special happens:&nbsp;<strong>the constraints depend on one another in a way that forms a closed loop</strong>. Constraint A enables process B, which produces structure C, which maintains constraint A.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is what distinguishes a cell from a crystal, an organism from a whirlpool. Crystals self-organize, but their constraints are imposed from outside. Whirlpools self-maintain, but only while external energy input continues in exactly the right way. Organisms are different: they regenerate the conditions for their own persistence. Their constraints close on themselves.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Alvaro Moreno and Mossio developed this into a full theory of biological autonomy (Moreno &amp; Mossio, 2015). Wim Hordijk, Mike Steel, and Stuart Kauffman's work on Reflexively Autocatalytic and Food-generated (RAF) sets shows how such closure can arise from chemistry without design (Hordijk, Steel &amp; Kauffman, 2012). The mathematics is rigorous. The biology is grounded. The philosophy follows.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Consciousness, whatever it is, emerges in systems with organizational closure.</strong>&nbsp;Not because closure magically produces experience, but because only systems with closure maintain the kind of boundaries, distinctions, and self-referential dynamics that could constitute experience in the first place. A camera lacks organizational closure. Its constraints are externally imposed and externally repaired. You have organizational closure. Your constraints regenerate one another. That difference matters.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.3 Ontic Structural Realism: Relations Without Relata</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>James Ladyman and Don Ross's 2007 book&nbsp;<em>Every Thing Must Go</em>&nbsp;makes a case that should be uncomfortable for anyone still attached to substances:&nbsp;<strong>at the fundamental level, there are no things. There are only relations</strong>&nbsp;(Ladyman &amp; Ross, 2007).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is ontic structural realism. Not merely the epistemic claim that we can only know structure (which is relatively safe), but the ontological claim that structure is all there is. Things are crystallizations of relations, not the other way around.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Steven French has developed the technical foundations (French, 2014). Carlo Rovelli's Relational Quantum Mechanics draws the same conclusion from physics: there are no observer-independent values, only relative facts (Rovelli, 1996). The measurement problem dissolves once you stop assuming there's a God's-eye view from which "the real state" could be specified.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>What does this mean for consciousness?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>There is no experiencer behind the experience.</strong>&nbsp;The "who" that philosophers keep asking about, the one who supposedly occupies the perspective, is a grammatical artifact. Search for the experiencer, and you find only more processes. This is not elimination of consciousness. It is dissolution of the assumption that consciousness requires an occupant.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Buddhism has known this for 2,500 years. Nagarjuna's&nbsp;<em>Mulamadhyamakakarika</em>&nbsp;systematically demonstrates that the self cannot be found as identical to the aggregates, different from them, possessing them, or possessed by them. The anatta doctrine is not nihilism. It is structural realism avant la lettre.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Identity emerges from constraint closure: what persists as "the same" is what regenerates its own constraints under perturbation. There is no further occupant to find, because persistence exhausts the explanatory role.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.4 Constraint Explanation vs Causal Explanation</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>A persistent confusion must be cleared before proceeding:&nbsp;<strong>constraint explanation is not causal explanation</strong>, and demanding causal answers to constraint questions is a category error.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Causal explanations answer: what produced this outcome? They trace event sequences: A pushed B, B moved C, C collided with D. They presuppose a space of possible trajectories and explain why one trajectory was taken rather than another.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Constraint explanations answer: why does this space of possibilities exist at all? They identify what rules out most configurations, leaving only a structured remainder. They do not trace sequences but specify the conditions under which sequences could occur.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Robert Rosen's work on relational biology makes this distinction rigorous.
