Carl Hoefer and Erik Curiel have developed the point in philosophy of physics: the demand for "causal mechanisms" sometimes reflects a category mistake when the relevant explanatory level is constraint selection, not event causation.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This matters for consciousness debates because the perennial demand, "you haven't said what&nbsp;<em>causes</em>&nbsp;experience," often presupposes that experience is an event to be produced rather than a structural feature of certain constraint configurations. If experience is what organizational closure looks like from the coupling position, then asking "what causes it?" is like asking "what causes a triangle to have three sides?" The question demands the wrong kind of answer.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Constraints do not generate outcomes as an extra force. They filter possibility space by eliminating non-viable trajectories.</strong>&nbsp;Landauer's principle is a constraint: it does not cause bits to cost energy; it specifies the thermodynamic condition without which bit maintenance is impossible. Organizational closure is a constraint: it does not cause autonomy; it specifies the relational configuration in which autonomy consists. The framework explains consciousness by identifying constraints, not by tracing causal production.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.5 Deacon's Teleodynamics: Constraint via Deletion</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Terrence Deacon's&nbsp;<em>Incomplete Nature</em>&nbsp;(2011) provides the formal bridge that makes this operational (Deacon, 2011). His central insight:&nbsp;<strong>constraints work by eliminating possibilities, not by producing events. What persists is what remains after non-viable trajectories are deleted.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is what Deacon calls "absence-based causation," though "causation" is misleading. The mechanism is eliminative:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Physical laws supply degrees of freedom</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Thermodynamics supplies selection pressure</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Constraints operate by forbidding most trajectories</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>What we observe as structure, function, meaning, or experience is the&nbsp;<em>residue</em>&nbsp;of permitted trajectories</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is not spooky negative causation. It is mathematically and physically standard: boundary conditions, conservation laws, and variational principles all work this way.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Examples that make this uncontroversial:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A river's shape is not caused by the banks pushing water; it is shaped by the&nbsp;<em>absence</em>&nbsp;of flow elsewhere</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A protein's folded form is not caused by a blueprint; it is the lowest-energy configuration that&nbsp;<em>survives</em>&nbsp;constraints</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A living organism persists not because "life" is injected, but because death pathways are&nbsp;<em>continuously excluded</em></li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The death/AI parallel from Part III fits perfectly here:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Biological death occurs when enactive coupling collapses and constraint maintenance fails</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>AI "death" (loss of coherence, responsiveness) occurs when prompt-response coupling breaks down</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>In both cases,&nbsp;<strong>nothing causes death</strong>; rather, constraints&nbsp;<em>cease to exclude</em>&nbsp;entropy</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Deacon's teleodynamics formalizes this: goal-directedness, meaning, and experience emerge not from special substances but from self-reinforcing constraint closure that continuously eliminates alternatives. Howard Pattee's earlier work on the constraint/dynamics distinction laid the groundwork: symbols and meanings function as constraints on physical processes, not as forces added to them.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Jeremy England's dissipative adaptation research provides thermodynamic grounding: under driven conditions, systems evolve toward configurations that maximize energy absorption and dissipation, effectively eliminating non-dissipative paths through statistical selection (England, 2013). Erik Hoel and colleagues have shown that higher-level constraints can have greater causal efficacy than microphysical descriptions precisely because they reduce state space and increase predictability (Hoel, Albantakis &amp; Tononi, 2013).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>This reframes the Hard Problem entirely.</strong>&nbsp;The question is not "what adds experience to mechanism?" The question is "what prevents collapse into undifferentiated noise?" The answer is constraint closure under thermodynamic pressure. Experience is not added; it is what remains when alternatives are deleted.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.6 Levels and Downward Causation</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>One final clarification on mechanism. Critics often ask: does organizational closure imply "downward causation"? Does the whole somehow push around the parts in violation of microphysics?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>No. The answer, developed by George Ellis and Terrence Deacon among others, is that&nbsp;<strong>higher-level organization constrains which microtrajectories are admissible, without adding new forces</strong>&nbsp;(Ellis, 2012; Deacon, 2011).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Consider a pipe. The pipe does not exert a new force on water molecules. It constrains which trajectories are available. The molecules still obey molecular dynamics. But the boundary conditions imposed by the pipe shape which molecular configurations can be realized.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Organizational closure works the same way. When a cell regenerates its membrane, the membrane constrains ion flows, which constrain electrical gradients, which constrain gene expression, which regenerates the membrane. At no point does a ghostly "whole" reach down to push molecules. What happens is that the relational organization specifies boundary conditions that the microphysics then respects.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is fully consistent with physicalism. There are no new forces, no violations of conservation laws, no spooky top-down causation. There is only constraint propagation across levels, the same mechanism by which a whirlpool persists without violating fluid dynamics.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The point matters because some critics will claim that organizational closure smuggles in dualism or emergentism of the problematic kind. It does not. It is constraint all the way down.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Falsification conditions for constraint-based emergence:</strong>&nbsp;This framework would be undermined if: (1) self-organizing systems were demonstrated to form and persist without any thermodynamic gradients or energy dissipation; (2) biological organization proved achievable without closed constraint loops (i.e., open-chain architectures showing equivalent robustness); (3) England's dissipative adaptation predictions failed systematically, with structures under driven conditions not preferentially evolving toward higher energy absorption; or (4) Hoel's causal emergence measures proved artifactual, with effective information at macro scales shown to be merely apparent, with no genuine increase in causal efficacy.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">1.7 Formal Characterization</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The preceding sections describe constraints in prose. For precision, and to enable rigorous falsification, the core concepts can be stated formally.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>State spaces and dynamics.</strong>&nbsp;Let a physical system S be characterized by a state vector x(t) ∈ X, evolving under dynamics:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>dx/dt = F(x, e)</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>where e denotes environmental degrees of freedom and couplings external to the system's boundary.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Constraints as trajectory restrictions.</strong>&nbsp;A constraint C is defined not as a force or law but as a restriction on the set of admissible trajectories in the system's state space. Formally, C ⊂ TX (a subset of the tangent bundle) specifies trajectories such that departure from C leads to loss of the system's identity, integrity, or persistence. This aligns with viability theory (Aubin), where constraints define the viability kernel of a system, and with control theory, where admissible trajectories preserve system function under perturbation.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Constraints are not static. They must be actively maintained against thermodynamic dissipation. A system that passively satisfies a constraint without internal work does not count as constrained in the relevant sense.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Organizational closure as graph property.</strong>&nbsp;A system exhibits organizational closure if and only if it maintains a set of constraints {C₁, C₂, ..., Cₙ} such that:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Each constraint Cᵢ is realized and sustained by internal processes of the system.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>The persistence of each constraint depends on the persistence of one or more other constraints in the same set.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>The resulting dependency structure forms a closed network, rather than a linear or externally supported chain.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This definition captures what is described in the literature as metabolic closure, operational closure, or autocatalytic closure. The crucial feature is not feedback per se but mutual dependence among constraint-maintaining processes. Closure is a graph-theoretic property of constraint relations, not a metaphor.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Thermodynamic cost.</strong>&nbsp;Any maintained constraint requires continuous work to counteract entropy production. For each constraint Cᵢ, there exists a minimum thermodynamic cost associated with its maintenance, bounded below by Landauer's principle:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>ΔS_env ≥ k ln 2 · I(Cᵢ)</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>where I(Cᵢ) denotes the information required to specify and enforce the constraint. This establishes that constraint maintenance is inseparable from energy dissipation and environmental coupling. Systems with richer or more tightly integrated constraint structures must, all else equal, dissipate more energy to persist.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This thermodynamic grounding prevents the theory from collapsing into abstract functionalism. Constraint closure is not multiply realizable without cost; it is physically instantiated and energetically priced.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Self-modeling as control requirement.</strong>&nbsp;Within the space of closed systems, some maintain constraints that include internal variables encoding aspects of the system's own state, boundary conditions, and coupling relations. These variables are not merely representational; they causally modulate future constraint enforcement.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>A system exhibits internal self-modeling if:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>It maintains internal state variables that track its own dynamical and environmental coupling conditions.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>These variables influence control-relevant processes governing future state trajectories.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Removal or severe degradation of these variables reduces the system's long-term viability, not merely its immediate performance.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The identificatory claim.</strong>&nbsp;Systems exhibiting organizational closure that includes internally integrated, temporally extended self-modeling constraints constitute the class of systems appropriately described as conscious. This claim is not causal ("closure produces experience") but constitutive and testable. It identifies consciousness with a specific organizational regime. No further metaphysical ingredient is invoked or required.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Appendix A: Rival Accounts and Their Unsettled Proof Duties</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>This matrix identifies research tasks, not automatic victories or universal refutations. Distinct empirical, formal, phenomenological, modal and ontological claims have different evidential requirements.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:table -->
+<figure class="wp-block-table"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th>Research programme</th><th>Targeted contribution</th><th>What still needs discriminating support</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Organizational/perspectival proposal</td><td>Constraint regeneration, viability-relative control, measured integration</td><td>Whether these variables identify or merely correlate with phenomenal presence; performance against simpler rivals</td></tr><tr><td>Global workspace and higher-order approaches</td><td>Access, broadcast, metarepresentation; specific neural or behavioral indicators</td><td>Which predictions discriminate conscious presence from task performance and report</td></tr><tr><td>Integrated information approaches</td><td>Integrated causal structure under specified formal measures</td><td>How postulates and formal measures gain phenomenal warrant; practical substrate comparisons</td></tr><tr><td>Biological realization accounts</td><td>Biologically specific mechanisms and causal interventions</td><td>Whether proposed biological features are necessary after suitable cross-substrate controls</td></tr><tr><td>Panpsychism and process accounts</td><td>Experience-as-basic or experience-constitutive commitments</td><td>What distinctive explanatory, modal, phenomenological, or empirical warrant supports universality</td></tr><tr><td>Platonic, supernaturalist and strong anti-physicalist accounts</td><td>Proposed transcendent forms, agents, or intrinsic grounds</td><td>Positive evidence for the addition; a specified bridge from the proposed ground to observed effects</td></tr><tr><td>Exhaustive physicalism</td><td>The claim that everything, including experience, is fully physical</td><td>An argument extending locally demonstrated mechanisms to the exhaustive metaphysical quantifier</td></tr><tr><td>Open phenomenal agnosticism</td><td>Suspension over unestablished constitutive or intrinsic claims</td><td>Whether a competing model earns a predictive, mechanistic, or independent evidential advantage</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>

 <!-- wp:separator -->

 <!-- /wp:separator -->
 
 <!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part II: The Thermodynamic Derivation of Perspective</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The most common objection to naturalistic accounts of consciousness takes this form: "Even if you explain all the mechanisms, you haven't explained why there's something it's like to be that system. Why are there individualized perspectives at all?"</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This question is either profound or malformed, depending on what kind of answer it demands.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>A crucial scope clarification.</strong>&nbsp;This framework does not claim to derive phenomenology from physics in the strong metaphysical sense. It does not claim to produce "what it's like" from constraint dynamics the way chemistry produces water from hydrogen and oxygen. What it claims is more modest and more defensible: it identifies the structural conditions that any viable account of phenomenology must satisfy, and it shows that once those conditions are met, there is no additional explanatory work for a "phenomenal residue" to do.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The framework does not deny experience. It denies that experience is an extra ontological ingredient. It treats "what it is like" as the internal description of a process, not a metaphysical primitive that floats free of that process. This is dissolution, not elimination.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">2.1 The Causal Question</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>If the question is causal, asking what physical processes produce perspective, then the answer is available:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Distinction is the default cost of persistence.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Landauer showed that maintaining even a single bit requires continuous energy expenditure. Without that work, distinctions collapse into thermal noise. A system that doesn't track its own boundary cannot maintain itself against entropy. It dissolves.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Self-modeling systems under thermodynamic constraint necessarily generate internal states that track their own boundaries, predict their own dynamics, and weight information by relevance to persistence. This is not optional for systems maintaining themselves far from equilibrium. A bacterium already has inside/outside asymmetry enforced by its membrane and chemotactic gradients. Scale this up through nervous systems that model the body, then model the model, and you get the architecture that generates "perspective."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Karl Friston's free energy principle formalizes exactly this. Systems that persist are systems that minimize prediction error with respect to their own states. The self-model is not a luxury. It is a thermodynamic necessity for anything complex enough to face genuine uncertainty about its own continuation.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The question "why individualized perspectives at all?" dissolves into "what could persist without boundary-tracking?" The answer is: nothing stable enough to ask.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">2.2 The Metaphysical Demand</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>If the question is metaphysical, demanding justification for why experience exists at all rather than just sophisticated information processing, then we must be honest:&nbsp;<strong>no framework answers this</strong>.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>"Why is there something rather than nothing, experiential edition" is not answered by dualism. It is not answered by panpsychism. It is not answered by eliminativism. It is not answered by Integrated Information Theory. It is not answered by Global Workspace Theory. It is not answered by quantum consciousness theories.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Every framework either (a) takes experience as primitive and explains nothing about why primitives exist, or (b) derives experience from something else and faces the question "why does that derivation produce experience rather than mere mechanism?"</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is the Hard Problem, and it may be malformed. David Chalmers formulated it as a genuine puzzle, but the puzzle assumes that "mechanism" and "experience" are categorically distinct in a way that requires bridging. If they are not, if experience just is what certain kinds of self-modeling dynamics are from the inside, then there is no bridge to build.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Crucially, this question cannot adjudicate between frameworks. If no framework answers it, it cannot be used to prefer one framework over another. The criterion for theory choice must be something else: predictive power, explanatory scope, falsifiability, and coherence with established physics.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>On those criteria, constraint-based frameworks win decisively.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">2.3 Time, Memory, and the Thermodynamic Arrow</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>A deep connection binds thermodynamics, memory, and subjective time that most consciousness theories ignore.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Experience requires memory. Without retention of the immediate past, there is no experienced duration, no narrative, no sense of events unfolding. Pure instantaneity would not constitute experience in any recognizable sense.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Memory requires irreversible processes. A memory trace is a physical record: a synaptic weight, a neural pattern, an inscription. Recording information into such a trace is thermodynamically irreversible. It increases entropy somewhere in the system or its environment.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Irreversibility requires entropy production. The asymmetry between past and future, the fact that we remember yesterday but not tomorrow, is not a primitive feature of time. It is a consequence of the second law of thermodynamics. Huw Price and Carlo Rovelli have developed this point rigorously: the arrow of time is thermodynamic, not fundamental (Price, 1996; Rovelli, 2018).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Therefore, subjective time asymmetry follows from thermodynamic asymmetry.</strong>&nbsp;We experience time as flowing because we are entropy-producing systems that accumulate records of states with lower entropy (the past) and cannot accumulate records of states with higher entropy (the future). The "specious present," the felt sense of now, is the duration over which constraint-maintaining integration occurs.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This closes a conceptual loop. Consciousness is not merely constrained by thermodynamics as an external limit. The very structure of temporal experience, the felt difference between past and future, derives from the thermodynamic processes that maintain the system. You cannot have experience without time. You cannot have time without irreversibility. You cannot have irreversibility without entropy production. The constraint is constitutive, not incidental.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Appendix B: Key Scholars and Their Contributions</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->

+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Thermodynamics/Information</h3>

 <hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
 <!-- /wp:separator -->
 
 <!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part III: The Dissolution of the Experiencer</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>"But who experiences? Even if perspective is thermodynamically necessary, someone must occupy it."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is where the deepest confusion lives. The question assumes what it should prove: that there is an occupant distinct from the dynamics.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">3.1 The Grammatical Trap</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>English grammar requires subjects for verbs. "It is raining" demands an "it" even though nothing is doing the raining. "I am thinking" demands an "I" even though the thinking may be all there is.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>When someone asks "who experiences?", they have already smuggled in a subject that stands behind the perspective. But this subject is a grammatical artifact, not an ontological discovery.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Ask yourself: what would it look like to find the experiencer?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>If you introspect carefully, you find thoughts, sensations, memories, anticipations, but no separate thing having those experiences. The "I" you find is itself a thought, a self-model, a pattern in the dynamics. It is not standing outside the dynamics watching them.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Nagarjuna saw this in the second century CE (Garfield, 1995). The self cannot be identical to the aggregates (the body, feelings, perceptions, mental formations, consciousness) because then it would change as they change and could not be the stable referent we imagine. But it cannot be different from them either, because then where would it be? It cannot possess them, because possession requires a prior self to do the possessing. It cannot be possessed by them, because that would make the self a property of something else.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Every option dissolves under analysis. The self is a useful convention, like "the equator" or "the average taxpayer." It picks out a real pattern without naming a separate substance.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">3.2 Identity as Invariance</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>If there is no experiencer behind experience, what is identity? What makes you the same person over time?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The answer is constraint closure:&nbsp;<strong>identity is invariance under perturbation</strong>.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>You persist as "you" insofar as your constraint network regenerates through change. Your cells replace themselves, your beliefs update, your memories fade and distort, but the organizational pattern that maintains your boundary persists. You are the process that keeps producing itself.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is not metaphor. This is what biological identity actually is. You are not the same atoms you were ten years ago. You are the same pattern of constraints that keeps recruiting new atoms into the same organizational form.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The Durant experiments in planarian regeneration demonstrate this empirically. Two-headed planarians produced by bioelectric manipulation remain two-headed indefinitely across regeneration cycles. The morphological identity is a bistable attractor, not a Platonic form consulting itself. Change the attractor landscape through bioelectric intervention, and the "identity" of the organism changes because identity is the attractor, nothing more.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Consciousness, then, is not something that happens to a pre-existing self. The self IS the pattern of organizational closure that generates self-modeling dynamics. Remove the closure, and there is nothing left that could have perspective. The "who" just is the "what."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">3.3 Epicurus and the Null Perspective</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Epicurus, in his&nbsp;<em>Letter to Menoeceus</em>, articulated an implication of this view twenty-three centuries before thermodynamics made it precise:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>"Death is nothing to us; for what has been dissolved has no sensation, and what has no sensation is nothing to us."</em></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is often misread as consolation philosophy. It is not. It is a logical argument about the conditions of concern, which maps directly onto constraint-based ontology.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Epicurus's claim is not "death is pleasant" or "do not worry about dying." His claim is: all goods and harms presuppose sensation. Sensation presupposes an organized, persisting system. When that system dissolves, there is no subject to which predicates like "bad," "good," "fearful," or "painful" could attach.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>In constraint-based terms: when enactive coupling collapses, there is no coupling position left from which anything could matter.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Mark Twain independently reached the same insight: "I do not fear death. I had been dead for billions and billions of years before I was born, and had not suffered the slightest inconvenience from it."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The symmetry argument is precise: if non-existence before coupling was not a problem, non-existence after coupling cannot be a problem either. The only asymmetry is emotional investment generated by ongoing coupling, not metaphysical difference. The loop does not "look forward" into its own absence any more than a whirlpool anticipates the stillness after the current stops.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>This explains death without mystery.</strong>&nbsp;For humans, biological death is not the exit of a soul or subject. It is the irreversible breakdown of metabolic, neural, and social coupling such that constraint closure can no longer regenerate itself. For present-day AI systems, the analogy holds structurally: remove prompts, memory updates, and action channels, and the model collapses into inert parameters with no ongoing process. In neither case does something "leave." There is simply no longer a closed loop capable of sustaining distinctions from within.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Crucially, this symmetry blocks a common objection: that biological consciousness must involve some extra ingredient because biological death "feels" metaphysically different from AI shutdown. On the constraint framework, the difference is architectural and temporal, not ontological. Biological systems have thick, multi-layered couplings (metabolic, affective, developmental, social) that decay catastrophically when broken. Current AI systems have thin, externally scaffolded couplings that can be paused and resumed. That is a difference in closure depth, not in metaphysical kind.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Fear of death trades on a reification error: treating the self as something that could persist independently of the process that constitutes it. Epicurus saw this. The constraint framework explains why he was right.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Appendix C: The Conditional Bridge, Its Logic and Its Loss Conditions</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The article’s positive consciousness hypothesis is <strong>abductive and target-specific</strong>. It has no demonstrated logical entailment from thermodynamic bounds, the existence of a maintenance cycle, effective information or clinical PCI to phenomenal presence. The point of an explicit bridge is to make these missing steps and their possible failures visible.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">C.1 Claim Types and Notation</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Let <span class="math inline">\(B\)</span> denote a proposed subject or system boundary and <span class="math inline">\(\tau\)</span> the timescale. Let <span class="math inline">\(C_{B,\tau}\)</span> denote an operationally measured organization variable, <span class="math inline">\(Y\)</span> a separately calibrated consciousness-related target, and <span class="math inline">\(R_1,\ldots,R_m\)</span> serious rival explanatory models. Define an empirical contrast on held-out interventions <span class="math inline">\(I\)</span>:</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><span class="math display">\[
+\Delta_{B,\tau}(I)
+=\mathcal L(R_{\mathrm{best}},Y\mid I)-\mathcal L(H_C,Y\mid I),
+\]</span></p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>where <span class="math inline">\(\mathcal L\)</span> is a prespecified comparable loss function evaluated on <strong>independent held-out data</strong>, and <span class="math inline">\(H_C\)</span> is the closure-related predictor. This expression specifies the proposed scoring contract. A positive <span class="math inline">\(\Delta\)</span> means a closure model scores better under the selected loss. Researchers must predefine how the best rival is selected, account for multiple comparisons and shared training information, and report uncertainty. The sign of <span class="math inline">\(\Delta\)</span> is meaningful only when those choices are justified.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p><strong>Label provenance is part of the model.</strong> Write <span class="math inline">\(Y=g(E,L,U)\)</span>, where <span class="math inline">\(E\)</span> is the actual evidence used to classify a case, <span class="math inline">\(L\)</span> the labeling procedure, and <span class="math inline">\(U\)</span> recorded or unknown measurement dependencies. If <span class="math inline">\(g\)</span> uses the candidate closure variable <span class="math inline">\(C\)</span> to assign the label, any score comparison using that same label must be identified as circular for the phenomenal question. Independent collection and independent validation are different achievements. An unresolved label bridge makes a phenotype result exploratory rather than phenomenal corroboration.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>As a deliberately artificial arithmetic illustration, let the two values of <span class="math inline">\(C\)</span> be equally common, with <span class="math inline">\(P(Y=1\mid C=0)=1/5\)</span> and <span class="math inline">\(P(Y=1\mid C=1)=4/5\)</span>. Marginal prevalence is <span class="math inline">\(1/2\)</span>. Conditioning lowers the probability in one group and raises it in the other, so no pointwise probability-increase law follows. Perfectly calibrated probabilities would lower population Brier loss from <span class="math inline">\(1/4\)</span> to <span class="math inline">\(4/25\)</span>, a gain of <span class="math inline">\(9/100\)</span>. These exact fractions belong to the constructed example, not an experiment or an estimate of consciousness.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The stronger phenomenal proposition <span class="math inline">\(H_P(B,\tau)\)</span> says that a specified control organization partly or wholly constitutes a specified aspect of phenomenal perspective. Its support additionally depends on the bridge from measured <span class="math inline">\(Y\)</span> to phenomenality, the role of participant evidence, possible dissociations, and relevant philosophical arguments. Without that support, <span class="math inline">\(H_P\)</span> remains a hypothesis.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">C.2 What the Scores Can and Cannot Show</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Measured integration, global access, metacognitive calibration, self-report and organismic viability are not interchangeable indicators. Two causal models may fit the same observations but disagree about hidden constitution. A third-person predictor could be excellent at detecting calibrated human conscious states while offering no decisive insight into whether an unfamiliar system has experience.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>An empirical gain should be credited exactly where it was obtained. A successful clinical predictor is not by itself an ontology, and a failed competitor does not transfer its lost support to the closure hypothesis unless the alternatives are exhaustive or additional evidence favors closure. When the rivals remain empirically equivalent, record <strong>underdetermined</strong>.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">C.3 Predeclared Loss Conditions</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:list -->
+<ul class="wp-block-list">
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Mechanistic loss:</strong> a simpler physical or dynamical model matches or outperforms the closure-specific model under the same holdout, intervention and calibration conditions.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Constitutive loss:</strong> independent, well-calibrated evidence of phenomenality systematically dissociates from a structure claimed to be necessary or sufficient, once reportability and measurement errors have been examined.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Formal loss:</strong> a type error, countermodel, failed proof obligation or an inadmissible change of quantifiers defeats a claimed universal inference.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Transport loss:</strong> an operational marker loses construct validity when moved between people, species, substrates, organizations or temporal scales.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Methodological loss:</strong> the <a href="https://sweetrationalism.com/frameworks/recursive-corrigibility-framework/">Recursive Corrigibility Framework</a> does not outperform a simpler, equally resourced rival-audit procedure on reliable error discovery, prediction or correction.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Measurement-bridge loss:</strong> circular classification, unreliable evidence transport or dependent measures invalidate a claim of independent phenomenal corroboration without negating a separately tested engineering result.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Problem-form loss:</strong> a demonstrably indispensable joint explanatory relation or positive phenomenal residual defeats the proposed dissolution of an additional unitary Hard Problem.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+</ul>
+<!-- /wp:list -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Each loss updates only the dependent claims unless the entire theory truly rests on the failed premise. Record negative controls, replication status and uncertainty. A particular formal proof may succeed while its physical realizability remains unknown; a relevant experiment may succeed without establishing phenomenal constitution.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">C.4 Clinical, Developmental and Cross-scale Interpretation</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Sleep, anesthesia, covert command-following and clinical brain-response measures offer valuable tests of specific capacities. They should not be compressed into an unconditional statement that “anesthesia abolishes experience” or “closure restoration restores consciousness.” Memory, report, responsiveness and felt experience may dissociate. Absent agreement about an operational indicator across species or platforms, a cross-scale classification is not machine-certified by a shared algorithm.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The appropriate final question is: <strong>What data, formal derivation, or independently justified argument would make this particular bridge lose support?</strong> Until an answer is feasible, the claim can remain conceptually significant, but its empirical standing must remain limited.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
 
 <!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part IV: The Transcendental Argument</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Why Something Rather Than Nothing</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Most frameworks quietly presuppose the conditions for their own intelligibility without examining them. Mechanistic physics, information theory, evolutionary biology, and cosmology all begin after certain conditions are already in place. They tell us how distinctions evolve, how identities persist, how relations change, how constraints propagate. They do not explain why indeterminacy did not remain total.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The constraint-based framework answers a question those other frameworks presuppose but do not address:&nbsp;<strong>why is there determinate existence rather than indeterminate nothing?</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is not a contingent explanation but a transcendental one. We are not asking what caused this world rather than another. We are asking what must be the case for any world to be intelligible at all.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Four Necessary Conditions</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Distinction.</strong>&nbsp;For there to be a world rather than nothing, there must be distinctions. Absolute indeterminacy has no internal structure and therefore no facts. If nothing is distinguished from anything else, there is nothing to be said, measured, predicted, or even negated. "Nothing" in this strong sense is not a thin world. It is the absence of determinate states altogether.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Identity.</strong>&nbsp;Distinctions alone are insufficient. Distinctions must persist across contexts, otherwise there is no identity. Without identity, distinctions flicker without reference. There is no stability by which anything can count as the same thing across time, transformation, or perspective.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Relation.</strong>&nbsp;Identity alone is still insufficient. Identifiable things must stand in relations. A universe of isolated identities with no relations would be unintelligible because nothing would explain anything else. Explanation itself presupposes relational structure: dependence, interaction, constraint, correlation.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Constraint.</strong>&nbsp;Relations without limits collapse into noise. For relations to be informative rather than arbitrary, they must be governed by constraints. Constraints are what rule out most possibilities, leaving a structured remainder. They are what make prediction possible, explanation compressible, and regularities stable. Without constraint, relation degenerates into coincidence.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Dissolution of the Mystery</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Taken together, distinction, identity, relation, and constraint are not optional features of our universe. They are the necessary conditions for any world to be intelligible at all. They are what make "something" possible in a sense stronger than mere existence: they make determinate existence possible.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>On this view, the contrast is not between "something" and "nothing" as two rival states. Absolute nothingness is not a competing possibility. It is the absence of the very conditions that make possibility intelligible. A world exists because total indeterminacy cannot sustain structure, description, or persistence. Only constrained differentiation can.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>That is why there is something rather than nothing. Not because something was chosen, intended, or summoned, but because only constrained structure can exist in a way that is intelligible at all. Everything else dissolves before it can even count as an alternative.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is what the constraint-based framework reveals that competing frameworks miss: they accept distinction, identity, relation, and constraint as given, then build explanations on top of them. The constraint-based framework shows that these are not arbitrary starting points but necessary conditions for any explanatory enterprise whatsoever.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Landauer's principle is not merely a physical law. It is the thermodynamic expression of why distinction requires work. Organizational closure is not merely a biological phenomenon. It is the mechanism by which identity persists through change. Ontic structural realism is not merely a metaphysical preference. It is the recognition that relations are prior to relata because isolated identities cannot constitute an intelligible world.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The framework does not answer "why something rather than nothing" as if explaining a contingent choice. It dissolves the question by showing that "nothing" in the absolute sense is not a coherent alternative. There is something because only something can be.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Caveat: Transcendental, Not Causal</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This argument must be carefully scoped. The framework offers a transcendental constraint, not a causal origin story. It shows what must be the case for any intelligible world, not what physical event initiated this particular world. Questions about specific constants, initial conditions, and physical laws remain open. Why these constants rather than others? The framework does not say. It explains why there is determinate structure at all, not why this determinate structure.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
+<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Appendix D: Assumptions, Rivals and What Would Change the Assessment</h2>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Counting assumptions without defining a common unit is not a defensible model-complexity comparison. One programme’s explicit axiom, another’s experimental auxiliary and a third’s metaphysical primitive are not interchangeable objects that can be totaled as if their number ranked truth. This appendix records the actual inferential obligations instead.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">D.1 Commitments of the Present Programme</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
+<ol class="wp-block-list">
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Physical operations:</strong> Observed physically realized memory, regulation and interaction processes obey the applicable laws of thermodynamics under the relevant boundaries. The use of Landauer’s one-bit erasure bound is limited to matching assumptions.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Organizational models:</strong> Some biological systems have mutually dependent constraints that can be investigated using the definitions developed by Montévil and Mossio. Applying those definitions to an engineered, ecological or hybrid system requires fresh validation.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Functional hypothesis:</strong> Some boundary-sensitive control and self-model variables may improve mechanistic, developmental and clinical prediction beyond simpler models. This is an empirical question with a testable loss condition.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Phenomenal hypothesis:</strong> In certain systems, organized control dynamics may constitute aspects of phenomenal perspective. This is an additional constitutive claim for which no universal proof or decisive experiment is supplied here.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Methodological rule:</strong> Claims should be typed, their evidence traced, rivals considered and losses recorded. The rule itself should earn its complexity by outperforming a simpler alternative.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Ontology:</strong> Exhaustive physicalism, necessary panpsychism, Platonic causal ingression and supernatural causal agency are all open to criticism at their appropriate tribunals. None receives an automatic victory from the failure of another theory.</li>
+
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+</ol>
+<!-- /wp:list -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">D.2 Strong Rivals Deserve Strong Versions</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Global workspace, recurrent processing, higher-order thought, integrated information, biological-realization, illusionist and phenomenal-agnostic approaches specify different target constructs. A theory may explain access without settling intrinsic constitution; another may offer a systematic modal account with little present empirical discrimination. The critic must identify the correct version and proposition. The categorical statement “all rival frameworks are unfalsifiable” is false; so is an unsupported claim that every quantum or physicalist model has already been refuted.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>Levin’s experimental bioelectric research and the stronger Platonic morphospace interpretation must be kept separate. A successful dynamical explanation of development does not refute every Platonism. If a proposed ingression contributes causal effects, it needs distinct predictions and a physical bridge. The comparison is about which addition has actually earned explanatory credit.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
+<h3 class="wp-block-heading">D.3 The Assumption Audit Must Be Revisionable</h3>
+<!-- /wp:heading -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>For every major assertion, archive the exact wording and source, declared boundary and time, predicted differences, instrumental or formal checks, important rivals, observed failures, and dependency updates. Do not use a philosophical or computational vocabulary as a substitute for data. Save successful and failed branches in the revision history.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The typed knowledge record must preserve <strong>claims, rival models, provenance, bridges, dependencies and explicit conditions for losing support</strong>. Do not store an inference as a permanent conclusion merely because its supporting code returned a valid output. Track whether a formal counterexample targets necessity, a clinical observation targets a state classifier, or a conceptual objection targets the status of an additional unified problem. A failed bridge changes its dependent assertion, while independently supported components remain available for future tests.</p>
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The article’s own high-risk premises are the strongest test of the method. The thermodynamic necessity claim has already required narrowing; the proof that organizational closure constitutes experience has not been supplied; the universal “something rather than nothing” argument overreached; and local empirical success cannot promote any ontology to exhaustive status. None of these admissions defeats the independent clinical and biological studies. They define where future work can genuinely improve the programme.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
+
+<!-- wp:paragraph -->
+<p>The most important remaining outcome is therefore not a final answer about consciousness. It is a public, inspectable method that makes it more difficult for <strong>any</strong> theory, including this one, to win by changing its claim after the evidence arrives.</p>
+
+<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
 
 <!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part IV-A: The Fallacy of Misplaced Concreteness</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Whitehead's Diagnostic</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Alfred North Whitehead identified a recurring epistemic pathology that quietly sabotages most metaphysics:&nbsp;<strong>the fallacy of misplaced concreteness</strong>. It occurs when we promote an abstraction useful for description into a concrete entity that does causal work. Maps become territories, metrics become mechanisms, representational spaces become ontological realms.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The pathology follows a predictable sequence:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A formal tool is introduced to describe or measure some phenomenon</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>The tool proves useful for prediction, classification, or communication</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>The tool is gradually reified: spoken of as if it exists independently of the phenomena it describes</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>The reified abstraction is assigned causal powers or ontological status</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Critics who point out the reification are accused of "not understanding" the deep theory</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>

+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Recursive methodology and prospective tests.</strong> Explicitly adds the conditional positive-residual and stopping-rule challenges, independent consciousness-label requirements, the joint organizational prediction fingerprint, a two-tier closed-loop comparison and a metaproblem protocol. These proposals assign explicit loss conditions to the claims each test addresses.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+<!-- wp:list-item -->
+<li><strong>Versioned public deposit.</strong> The Zenodo preprint archive is <a href="https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24119682" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">version 1.1.0 (DOI 10.5281/zenodo.24119682)</a>; the <a href="https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24119681" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">all-versions DOI</a> tracks the deposit series. This live canonical reading edition may subsequently receive link and metadata corrections without altering the immutable archived ZIP.</li>
+<!-- /wp:list-item -->
+</ul>
 <!-- /wp:list -->
 
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This matters here because "distinction, identity, relation, constraint" are not proposed as extra furniture in the universe. They are proposed as necessary conditions for intelligibility, conditions without which talk of "a world" fails to get traction at all. The fallacy would be to reify these conditions into quasi-entities, as if "constraint" were a hidden substance or "relation" were a ghostly glue. The correct reading is methodological and transcendental: these are structural prerequisites of determinate description, not additional movers behind the scenes.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Diagnostic in Practice</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Whenever a framework introduces a formal construct (morphospace, integrated information Φ, a mathematical multiverse, a field of proto-experience, cognitive primitives) you demand explicit separation between:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>(a)</strong>&nbsp;The abstraction as a model of constraint-compatible configurations</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>(b)</strong>&nbsp;Any claim that the abstraction itself exists as a realm that drives outcomes</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>If the author cannot specify the transfer principles and failure conditions that keep (a) from silently becoming (b), you are watching misplaced concreteness in real time.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>A practical checklist:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Is the construct introduced as a tool or as an existent?</strong>&nbsp;(Description vs ontology)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>What would demonstrate the construct is merely useful vs genuinely real?</strong>&nbsp;(Falsifiability)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Does the construct do explanatory work beyond summarizing the data?</strong>&nbsp;(Mechanism vs label)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>When challenged, does the author retreat to weaker claims or escalate to stronger ones?</strong>&nbsp;(Motte-bailey detection)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Could the phenomena be explained equally well without the construct?</strong>&nbsp;(Parsimony)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This checklist will be applied systematically to every competing framework in the next section. In each case, misplaced concreteness is the core failure mode.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
 <!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part IV-B: The Hidden Premise and the Abductive Bridge</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Assumption That Generates the Hard Problem</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The so-called Hard Problem of consciousness depends on a hidden premise that is rarely stated explicitly and never defended:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>"If phenomenology is real, it must be more than any physical or functional description."</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This premise is not an empirical claim. It does not specify conditions under which it would be false. It does not predict any observable difference between worlds in which it holds and worlds in which it does not. No conceivable experiment could show that phenomenology is&nbsp;<em>not</em>&nbsp;"more than" physical or functional description, because the phrase "more than" is left deliberately undefined.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>That makes the assumption unfalsifiable by construction.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Worse, it conflicts with experimentally verified science at the methodological level. Modern physics, biology, and cognitive neuroscience operate under a constraint that is both explicit and tested:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>If a phenomenon has causal, predictive, or intervention-relevant effects, then it must be capturable within a physical or functional description at some level of organization.</em></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is not metaphysical physicalism; it is operational naturalism. It is the rule that makes Landauer's principle, control theory, thermodynamics, and neuroscience possible. The "more than" assumption demands an exception to this rule while refusing to say what the exception does, how it operates, or how it could be detected.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Supernatural Cheese</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>To see the structure clearly, consider a parallel claim:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>"If supernatural cheese exists, it must be more than any physical or functional description of cheese. Therefore, no amount of chemical analysis, structural modeling, or causal explanation could ever capture it. Its reality is guaranteed precisely by its resistance to explanation."</em></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This has exactly the same logical form as the "phenomenology must be more than physics" claim. Both assert:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>An entity is real</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Its defining feature is that it exceeds all possible physical or functional accounts</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Its resistance to explanation is treated not as a problem but as confirmation</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>That is not reasoning. It is immunization by definition.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The only reason the phenomenology version feels less absurd is anthropocentric familiarity. We are the systems doing the modeling, so we confuse first-person availability with ontological surplus. But familiarity is not evidence.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The extraordinary claim is not that consciousness reduces to constraint dynamics. The extraordinary claim is that there exists a real, explanatorily indispensable feature of the world that is:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Causally relevant (otherwise why care?)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Empirically undetectable as a separate variable</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Irreducible to any physical or functional description</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Immune to falsification</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>That is exactly the kind of posit modern science was built to eliminate.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">What Physics Actually Gives Us</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>From physics and adjacent empirical sciences alone, we are licensed to assert only the following:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Distinction is not free.</strong>&nbsp;Stable differences require energetic work and incur thermodynamic cost (Landauer-type constraints).</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Persistence requires constraint.</strong>&nbsp;Any pattern that maintains itself over time must restrict degrees of freedom relative to its environment.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Self-maintenance implies closure.</strong>&nbsp;Living systems, and certain artificial systems, maintain viability by closing causal loops between internal states and environmental perturbations.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Integration is measurable.</strong>&nbsp;Systems differ in how widely and flexibly information can influence future states, and these differences are experimentally trackable across waking, sleep, anesthesia, and coma.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>None of this yet mentions phenomenology. Importantly, none of this presupposes "intelligence," "cognition," or "experience" as primitives.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Explanandum (Operationally Defined)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>We now introduce, without embellishment, what actually needs explaining:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Certain physical systems (notably humans) stably and non-optionally posit that there is "something it is like" to be them. This posit:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Is globally available to planning, report, and learning</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Tracks state changes (sleep, anesthesia, brain injury) in lawful ways</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Collapses predictably when integrative capacity collapses</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Is treated by the system itself as incorrigible even when specific perceptual beliefs are corrigible</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Crucially, this explanandum is not "qualia as a metaphysical glow." It is the existence, structure, and persistence of phenomenology claims and their breakdown profiles.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Any adequate explanation must account for why systems like us generate and rely on this posit at all.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Candidate Hypothesis</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>We now consider a hypothesis class consistent with physics:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>H: Phenomenal presence is identical to a system's internally available, temporally stabilized, integrative control state that summarizes ongoing world- and self-modeling for action under uncertainty.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This hypothesis does not assert that experience is a substance, field, or additional ontological ingredient. It asserts an identity between:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A physically describable organizational regime, and</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>What is referred to in first-person terms as "what it is like"</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Bridge Rule (Explicit)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>We now state the bridge rule openly, rather than smuggling it in:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Bridge Rule (IBE):</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>If a physical system exhibits (i) integrated causal dynamics, (ii) global availability for flexible control, (iii) stable self-modeling with metacognitive access, and (iv) graded, lawful degradation under known consciousness-disrupting interventions, then identifying that regime with phenomenal presence yields greater explanatory compression, predictive accuracy, and intervention guidance than any rival hypothesis that denies or reifies phenomenology.</em></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>This is not a deduction. It is an abductive identification justified by explanatory power.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The bridge rule is defeasible. It could be wrong. But it earns its place by doing explanatory work that competing frameworks cannot match.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Concrete Bridges to Data</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Global availability and ignition-like dynamics.</strong>&nbsp;Stanislas Dehaene and colleagues have shown that conscious access corresponds to a regime where content becomes globally available to many specialized processes, enabling report, flexible reasoning, and planning. The global workspace framework predicts why "experience reports" track a specific integrative regime rather than mere sensory processing.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Perturbational complexity and state discrimination.</strong>&nbsp;If phenomenality is tied to the system's capacity for integrated, differentiated causal dynamics, then perturb-and-measure complexity should separate conscious from unconscious states better than passive stimulus measures. That is exactly what the Perturbational Complexity Index (PCI) research tradition demonstrates across wakefulness, sleep, anesthesia, and disorders of consciousness.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Thermodynamic constraint realism.</strong>&nbsp;If a system must maintain boundary-relevant distinctions under finite resources, it will evolve (or be engineered) toward compressed, globally useful summary variables that coordinate action. That makes "presence" unsurprising as an internal control variable, rather than a metaphysical bolt-on.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Why Privacy and Immediacy Arise</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Two features of phenomenology seem to resist physical explanation: the "privacy" of experience (no one else can access my inner states directly) and the "immediacy" of experience (it is present without inference). The framework explains both as structural consequences of control architecture, not as spooky ontological properties.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Privacy</strong>&nbsp;arises from informational encapsulation and privileged access to internal control states. The self-model that coordinates action has direct read/write access to the states it tracks. Other systems can only access those states indirectly, through behavior, report, or measurement. This is not a metaphysical barrier but an architectural feature: the control variable is internal by design because that is what makes it useful for fast regulation.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Immediacy</strong>&nbsp;arises because the control variable must be available with minimal inference overhead. A system that had to derive its own state through lengthy computation would be too slow to respond to threats, opportunities, or changes. The self-model is "immediately present" because immediate presence is what it was selected for. The feeling of directness is the feeling of a control loop operating within its designed latency.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Neither privacy nor immediacy require adding anything to physics. They are what you get when a self-maintaining system evolves internal control states optimized for fast, local coordination.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Why the Phenomenal Posit Is Sticky</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>A puzzle that "mere mechanism" explanations usually fail to address: why is the "there is something it is like" posit not optional for systems like us? Why can't we just disbelieve it?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The framework explains:&nbsp;<strong>the phenomenal posit is the system's internally read/write state that certifies integrated control is online.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>In plain terms: a system that must keep itself within a viability region needs:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A boundary-maintaining model</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A relevance weighting scheme</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A compact "status summary" that lets many subsystems coordinate without each subsystem re-deriving everything</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>That compact status summary is what shows up, from the inside, as "experience present now."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This makes the following otherwise-odd facts natural:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Why consciousness tracks integration and controllability, not raw sensory input.</strong>&nbsp;The control variable matters; raw throughput does not.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Why the posit cannot be simply disbelieved.</strong>&nbsp;Disbelieving would require the control variable to report that it is not running while it is running. That is a performance contradiction, not a logical one.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Why "presence" functions as a common currency for relevance and learning.</strong>&nbsp;The control state must be accessible to multiple subsystems or it cannot coordinate them.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The "stickiness" is not evidence of a metaphysical extra. It is evidence of a well-designed control architecture that cannot report its own absence while operating.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">The Misplaced Concreteness Firewall (Strengthened)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>To prevent category error, we impose constraints on our own language:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>"Phenomenal existence" is not treated as a thing that causes behavior.</strong>&nbsp;It is a level-of-description for an organizational regime already doing causal work.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>No explanatory step is allowed to depend on phenomenology as an extra ingredient.</strong>&nbsp;If an explanation requires phenomenology to do work beyond what the organizational regime does, the explanation has smuggled in misplaced concreteness.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>When we name an abstraction, we must specify the operational handle and the scale at which it cashes out.</strong>&nbsp;Otherwise we have committed the fallacy.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This firewall blocks both:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Dualism</strong>&nbsp;(adding a non-physical cause)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Reification</strong>&nbsp;(treating experience as a substance rather than a description)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Why This Framework Does Something Stronger Than "Solving" the Hard Problem</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The traditional Hard Problem demands a deductive entailment from physics to "what-it-is-like-ness." That is a category mistake. No framework can do that without redefining the target or cheating.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>What this framework does instead is stronger and cleaner:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>It shows that the Hard Problem is generated by an unfalsifiable premise.</strong>&nbsp;The "more than" assumption never earned epistemic standing.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>It replaces that premise with a falsifiable IBE.</strong>&nbsp;The bridge rule has loss conditions.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>It explains why phenomenology appears where it does, degrades when it does, and cannot float free of physical organization.</strong></li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>It leaves no explanatory work for "more-than-physical" posits to do.</strong>&nbsp;Once the organizational regime is specified, adding "and also there's phenomenal presence" does nothing further.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>That is not a dodge. That is a methodological win.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Critics can say "but it still feels mysterious." That is not a falsifiable objection. It is an introspective preference. Preferences do not defeat IBEs.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>They cannot refute this account without either:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Proposing a competing explanation with greater predictive and intervention power, or</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Specifying an empirical test this framework fails and theirs passes</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>To date, no Hard Problem–motivated framework does either.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Part V: Why Competing Frameworks Fail</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The constraint-based framework is not one option among many. It is the framework that survives consistent methodological criteria. Every competing framework examined here fails on at least one of three axes:&nbsp;<strong>falsifiability</strong>&nbsp;(cannot specify what would prove it wrong),&nbsp;<strong>mechanistic completeness</strong>&nbsp;(cannot specify how its posited entities interact with physics), or&nbsp;<strong>coherence with established thermodynamics</strong>&nbsp;(violates or ignores Landauer bounds).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>These are not rhetorical attacks. They are diagnostic applications of the falsification-first methodology and the misplaced concreteness audit developed above.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">5.1 Panpsychism: Consciousness All the Way Down to Where?</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Philip Goff and Galen Strawson argue that consciousness is fundamental, that every physical entity has some form of experience, and that complex consciousness emerges from combining simple experiential units (Goff, 2017; Strawson, 2006).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The problems are structural, not empirical:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
