-<p><strong>Theorem 4.3 (Strict Dependence → Unfalsifiability):</strong> If prediction and inference data are <em>strictly dependent</em> — meaning a function exists that deduces one from the other — then the theory is empirically unfalsifiable. Experiments contribute nothing. The theory is tautological.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The substitution argument generalizes the "unfolding argument" that Doerig et al. (2019) deployed against IIT specifically: any recurrent neural network can be unfolded into a feedforward one with identical input-output function but radically different internal structure, so IIT must make different consciousness predictions for systems that are behaviorally indistinguishable. Kleiner and Hoel show this is not a problem with IIT alone. It affects every theory that predicts consciousness from internal observables while inferring it from reports. Universal computers, neural networks with different architectures, and lookup tables can all produce identical outputs from radically different internal states. The class of viable substitutions is enormous.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The escape routes Kleiner and Hoel identify are exactly two:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Lenient dependency:</strong> Prediction and inference data constrain each other without being independent or strictly dependent. They note: "No current theory or testing paradigm that we know of satisfies this definition."</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Abandoning causal closure of the physical:</strong> Allowing conscious experience itself to make a difference to physical dynamics beyond what physical information alone predicts. This is dualism by another name, and most researchers reject it.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This section proves that the organizational closure framework instantiates escape route (1) — and does so through three independent, mutually reinforcing mechanisms grounded in physics, biology, and information theory, none of which depend on the framework's own identity claims for their force.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.2 Formal Setup: Translating the Framework into Kleiner-Hoel Notation</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.2.1 Standard Kleiner-Hoel Notation</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Following their formalism precisely:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>P</strong> = class of physical systems that could be tested in the experiment<br><strong>O</strong> = class of all datasets resulting from observations/measurements of P<br><strong>obs</strong>: P → O (correspondence from physical systems to datasets)<br><strong>E</strong> = space of possible experiences specified by the theory<br><strong>pred</strong>: O → E (theory's prediction mapping from observables to experiences)<br><strong>inf</strong>: O → E (experimenter's inference mapping from reports to experiences)</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>For any dataset o ∈ O:<br><strong>o_i</strong> = prediction data (the part of o used by pred)<br><strong>o_r</strong> = inference data (the part of o used by inf)</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Falsification</strong> at dataset o occurs when pred(o_i) ∩ inf(o_r) = ∅.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Independence</strong> (Definition 3.8): For any o_i, o_i′, and o_r, there exists a physically realizable variation ν: P → P such that ν preserves o_r but changes o_i to o_i′.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Strict Dependence</strong> (Definition 4.2): There exists a function f such that for any dataset in the full possibility space, o_i = f(o_r).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.2.2 Organizational Closure Framework Instantiation</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>For the organizational closure framework, the Kleiner-Hoel variables instantiate as follows:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Prediction data (o_i)</strong> consists of three independently measurable classes of observables:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Thermodynamic observables (o_θ):</em></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Entropy production rate (σ̇), measurable via calorimetry or metabolic markers</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Distance from thermodynamic equilibrium (D_eq), measurable via broken detailed balance (Lynn et al. 2021)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Perturbational complexity index (PCI), measurable via TMS-EEG (Casarotto et al. 2016)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Effective connectivity asymmetry, measurable via generative effective connectivity models (Stikvoort et al. 2025)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Closure observables (o_c):</em></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Constraint regeneration patterns, measurable via perturbation-recovery protocols</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Autocatalytic cycle structure, measurable via chemical organization theory (Hordijk &amp; Steel 2015)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Boundary maintenance dynamics, measurable via self/nonself discrimination assays</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Autonomy markers, measurable via intervention studies (remove external support, measure persistence)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Structural observables (o_s):</em></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Network topology (as in standard neuroscience)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Integration/differentiation measures (as in IIT)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Global broadcasting patterns (as in GNW)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Inference data (o_r)</strong> consists of:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Verbal reports (when available)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Behavioral indicators (approach/avoid dynamics, differential responsiveness)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Autonomic correlates (pupil dilation, galvanic skin response, heart rate variability)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>No-report paradigm indicators (optokinetic nystagmus, binocular rivalry markers)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Computational output patterns (for artificial systems)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Experience space (E):</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>E = {(L, M, C) | L ∈ Levels, M ∈ Matterings, C ∈ Contents}</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>L = degree of organizational closure (scalar, tracking distance from equilibrium)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>M = mattering partition structure (which environmental states threaten, support, or are irrelevant to the system's closure)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>C = content structure (which specific distinctions are being maintained at thermodynamic cost)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Prediction function (pred):</strong> pred(o_i) = pred(o_θ, o_c, o_s) = (L_predicted, M_predicted, C_predicted)</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Inference function (inf):</strong> inf(o_r) = (L_inferred, M_inferred, C_inferred)</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The question that determines the framework's fate: what is the dependency relationship between o_i and o_r?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.3 Three Independent Proofs of Lenient Dependency</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The organizational closure framework achieves lenient dependency through three independent mechanisms operating at different levels. Each alone constrains the substitution space. Together, they are mutually reinforcing in a way that addresses vulnerabilities any single mechanism leaves open. Crucially, the first mechanism is grounded entirely in physics and does not depend on any claim specific to this consciousness theory.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.3.1 Mechanism 1: The Landauer Constraint (Physics-Grounded)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Claim:</strong> The second law of thermodynamics and Landauer's principle impose a lawful physical coupling between thermodynamic prediction data (o_θ) and structured inference data (o_r) that breaks independence without establishing strict dependence.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>This mechanism does not depend on the organizational closure framework being correct.</strong> It is a consequence of physics that constrains any consciousness theory making thermodynamic predictions. It is presented first because it is the foundation — a non-negotiable constraint on the substitution space that holds regardless of one's theory of consciousness.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Proof:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>(A) Structured reports require thermodynamic work (lower bound).</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Any structured report — verbal, behavioral, or output-functional — constitutes a macroscopic reduction of entropy in the report channel. The report must distinguish among possible experiential states. By Landauer's principle (1961), erasing one bit of information requires dissipating at minimum kT ln 2 of energy. Producing a report that distinguishes among n experiential states requires encoding at minimum log₂(n) bits of information, which requires erasing at minimum log₂(n) bits from the report channel, costing at minimum kT ln 2 × log₂(n) joules.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>By the second law, the system producing the report must be performing this thermodynamic work, which requires being sufficiently far from thermodynamic equilibrium. A system at equilibrium performs no work and produces no structured reports (by definition: at equilibrium, all microstates are equally probable and no macroscopic distinctions are maintained).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Formally, let R(o_r) denote the Shannon information content of a structured report o_r (measured in bits). Let σ̇_system denote the entropy production rate of the system, and let Δt denote the time interval over which the report is produced. Then the Landauer constraint requires:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:code -->
-<pre class="wp-block-code"><code>σ̇_system ≥ R(o_r) × kT ln 2 / Δt                    &#91;Landauer Bound on Reports]</code></pre>
-<!-- /wp:code -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is not a theoretical speculation. It follows from the same physics that Bérut et al. (2012) confirmed experimentally for colloidal particles, Bryant and Machta (2023) measured for biological synapses, and Aimet et al. (2025) verified in quantum many-body regimes. No exceptions exist across any substrate yet tested.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>(B) This breaks independence.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Recall Kleiner-Hoel's Definition 3.8: independence requires that for <em>any</em> o_i and o_r, there exists a physically realizable variation ν that instantiates that pairing.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The Landauer Bound on Reports forbids an entire region of the (o_i, o_r) space:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:code -->
-<pre class="wp-block-code"><code>Forbidden region F = { (o_i, o_r) : R(o_r) &gt; 0 ∧ σ̇(o_i) &lt; R(o_r) × kT ln 2 / Δt }</code></pre>
-<!-- /wp:code -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>For any structured report o_r with R(o_r) &gt; 0, there exists a nonempty set of thermodynamic states o_i (namely, those at or near equilibrium) that cannot be physically paired with that report. The variation ν required by Definition 3.8 does not exist for these pairings — not because of our theory, but because of the second law of thermodynamics.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Therefore: o_i and o_r are not independent in Kleiner-Hoel's sense. The substitution space is physically constrained. □</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>(C) This does not establish strict dependence.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Strict dependence (Definition 4.2) requires a function f such that o_i = f(o_r) for all possible datasets. This fails in both directions:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Forward:</em> A system can have arbitrarily high entropy production (high σ̇ in o_θ) while producing minimal or null reports (low R(o_r)). A candle flame dissipates substantial energy while "reporting" nothing. A brain under deep anesthesia still consumes metabolic energy while reports cease. Knowing o_θ does not determine o_r.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Reverse:</em> Two systems producing identical reports can have vastly different entropy production rates. A biological brain and a silicon emulator producing the same verbal outputs have entropy production rates differing by orders of magnitude. Knowing o_r does not determine o_θ.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>No function f: o_r → o_i or f: o_i → o_r exists that holds across the space of physically realizable systems. Therefore: strict dependence fails. □</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>(D) Therefore: lenient dependency via physics.</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The Landauer Bound on Reports establishes that the (o_θ, o_r) subspace of (o_i, o_r) is leniently dependent. The prediction data and inference data constrain each other (reports require thermodynamic work; thermodynamic work is necessary but not sufficient for reports) without being reducible to each other.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Strength of this mechanism:</strong> Unassailable for any theory using thermodynamic prediction data. The constraint follows from established physics.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Limitation of this mechanism alone:</strong> The Landauer bound is a floor, not a ceiling. Above the floor, many distinct thermodynamic profiles remain compatible with any given report. The forbidden region F constrains the substitution space but does not eliminate it. A residual substitution space exists: the set of systems whose entropy production exceeds the Landauer bound for their reports but whose thermodynamic profiles differ in ways that generate different consciousness predictions.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is where Mechanisms 2 and 3 enter.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.3.2 Mechanism 2: The Closure-Mattering Entailment (Biology-Grounded)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Claim:</strong> Organizational closure thermodynamically entails differential mattering, creating a lawful coupling between closure observables (o_c) and behavioral inference data (o_r) that further constrains the substitution space beyond what Landauer alone achieves.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Unlike Mechanism 1, this mechanism does depend on the organizational closure framework's core claim. But the claim is independently testable, has 50+ years of empirical support from autonomous systems research, and specifies precise conditions under which it would fail.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Definitions:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Definition OC.1 (Organizational Closure):</strong> A physical system S exhibits organizational closure if and only if:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(i) <em>Constraint regeneration:</em> The constraints C₁, ..., Cₙ that delimit S's dynamics are produced and maintained by the processes those constraints enable. Formally: each Cᵢ is generated by processes Pⱼ that require other constraints Cₖ for their operation, forming a closed network where every constraint is both product and condition.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(ii) <em>Thermodynamic cost:</em> Maintaining each constraint requires continuous energy dissipation ≥ kT ln 2 per maintained distinction (Landauer bound). Total dissipation D = Σᵢ Dᵢ.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(iii) <em>Boundary maintenance:</em> S maintains a thermodynamic boundary distinguishing self from environment, requiring work W against entropy increase.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(iv) <em>Autonomy:</em> The closure persists because constraints regenerate each other's conditions (internal causation dominant), not merely because external conditions supply them.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>These are independently measurable: constraint regeneration via perturbation-recovery protocols (disable Cᵢ, measure whether other Cⱼ regenerate it); thermodynamic cost via calorimetry; boundary maintenance via distinction persistence under noise; autonomy via intervention studies.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Definition DM.1 (Differential Mattering):</strong> A system with organizational closure exhibits differential mattering if and only if:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(i) <em>Threat/Support Partitioning:</em> Environmental states partition into threatening (θ: increase probability of closure failure), supporting (σ: decrease probability of closure failure), and irrelevant (ι: negligible effect on closure probability).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(ii) <em>Differential Response:</em> The system's dynamics differ measurably across partitions: R(θ) ≠ R(σ) ≠ R(ι).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(iii) <em>Viability-Relativity:</em> The partition is defined relative to the specific closure being maintained, not absolutely.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This is measurable without requiring report: perturbation studies identify which states elicit approach/avoidance; autonomic correlates detect differential response; comparative studies across systems with different closures test viability-relativity.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Theorem OC.1 (Closure-Mattering Entailment):</strong> If organizational closure at level L exists and persists over time, differential mattering at level L must exist.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Proof:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(1) Organizational closure requires maintaining at minimum the self/nonself boundary distinction (Definition OC.1(iii)). By Landauer, this costs energy continuously.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(2) The closure exists in an environment whose states vary. Environmental states can be partitioned by their thermodynamic effect on the closure:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>States increasing internal entropy production beyond sustainable dissipation rates threaten the closure</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>States maintaining entropy production within sustainable bounds support the closure</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>States with negligible effect on internal entropy production are irrelevant</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This partition exists as a fact about the physics of the system-environment coupling, regardless of whether the system "detects" it.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(3) For the closure to persist (rather than undergo random dissolution), the system's dynamics must be differentially sensitive to this partition. Consider the contrapositive: if the system responds identically to threatening, supporting, and irrelevant states, its trajectory in state space is uncorrelated with boundary conditions on its viability. Over time, it will encounter threatening states and fail to respond appropriately. The probability of dissolution approaches 1 as time → ∞ (random walk in partition space hits the absorbing boundary of closure failure).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(4) Therefore, any organizational closure that persists exhibits differential response to the threat/support partition — which is differential mattering (Definition DM.1). The mattering is not an additional property of the closure. It is the closure's persistence described from the coupling position. □</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Corollary OC.2:</strong> Organizational closure without differential mattering is thermodynamically unstable and will not be observed in persistent systems. (Any such system would dissolve before observation.)</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>How this constrains substitutions:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Theorem OC.1 establishes a lawful relationship between o_c (closure observables) and a subset of o_r (differential responsiveness measures). This relationship is:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Not independent:</em> If a substitution changes the closure structure (changing o_c), the mattering partition must change correspondingly (changing the differential responsiveness component of o_r). You cannot substitute a system with genuine organizational closure for one without closure while preserving the <em>genuine</em> differential responsiveness pattern — because the responsiveness of a closed system is to threats against <em>its own closure</em>, and a system without closure has no closure to be threatened.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Not strictly dependent:</em> Multiple closure structures can produce similar (though not identical at fine grain) mattering partitions. E. coli and octopus both discriminate environmental threats from supports, but their closures differ radically. And o_c is measured by different experimental procedures than o_r (perturbation-recovery vs. behavioral observation).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Critical clarification — addressing the circularity objection:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>One might object: "You're using your theory's core claim (closure entails mattering) to prove your theory escapes falsification. This is circular."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This objection has force but is not fatal, for three reasons:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>First, Theorem OC.1 is independently falsifiable. Falsification criterion FC.1: find a system with genuine organizational closure (constraint regeneration measurable) but no differential responsiveness to boundary threats. This has been sought for 50+ years in autonomous systems research (Varela 1979; Jonas 1966; Maturana &amp; Varela 1980; Di Paolo 2005; Di Paolo et al. 2017; Kauffman 2019). No counterexample exists. The claim is not stipulated; it is empirically robust.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Second, the argument does not require Theorem OC.1 to establish lenient dependency. Mechanism 1 (Landauer) already establishes lenient dependency using physics alone. Mechanism 2 <em>strengthens</em> the lenient dependency by further constraining the above-floor substitution space. Even if Theorem OC.1 is wrong, Mechanism 1 still holds.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Third, every consciousness theory's predictions depend on some theoretical claim. IIT claims Φ determines consciousness; GNW claims global broadcasting determines consciousness. The question is not whether the claim is theory-dependent (it inevitably is) but whether it is independently testable. The Closure-Mattering Entailment is testable via FC.1. If it passes, the lenient dependency is strengthened. If it fails, the framework falls — but Mechanism 1 remains, and a replacement framework could inherit it.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Limitation of this mechanism:</strong> The entailment is from closure to mattering (one direction). The reverse direction is weaker: differential mattering suggests closure but does not logically guarantee it (a system could conceivably discriminate threats without full organizational closure, if "threat" were defined relative to some external standard rather than the system's own viability). This asymmetry means the substitution space is more constrained in one direction than the other.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.3.3 Mechanism 3: The Third Measurement Channel (Information-Theoretic)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Claim:</strong> The organizational closure framework's prediction data includes a class of thermodynamic observables (o_θ) that are measurable independently of both structural observables (o_s) and inference data (o_r), breaking Kleiner-Hoel's two-channel formalism and introducing a novel falsification structure that standard theories lack.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The two-channel assumption in Kleiner-Hoel:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The entire Kleiner-Hoel framework is built on a two-channel architecture:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:code -->
-<pre class="wp-block-code"><code>Dataset o = (o_i, o_r)
-  where o_i = prediction data (internal observables)
-        o_r = inference data (reports/behavior)</code></pre>
-<!-- /wp:code -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The substitution argument works because you can vary o_i while holding o_r fixed (if independent) or because o_i adds nothing beyond o_r (if strictly dependent). The dilemma arises from having exactly two channels with a binary relationship.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The three-channel architecture:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The organizational closure framework operates with three partially overlapping but independently accessible measurement channels:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:code -->
-<pre class="wp-block-code"><code>Dataset o = (o_θ, o_c, o_r)
-  where o_θ = thermodynamic observables (entropy production, PCI, broken detailed balance)
-        o_c = closure observables (constraint regeneration, boundary maintenance)
-        o_r = inference data (reports, differential responsiveness, behavior)</code></pre>
-<!-- /wp:code -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Crucially:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>o_θ is measurable without accessing o_r (entropy production can be estimated from neural dynamics alone: Perl et al. 2021; Lynn et al. 2021)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>o_θ is measurable without requiring the full structural model in o_c (PCI requires only TMS-EEG, not a complete constraint map)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>o_r is measurable without accessing o_θ (standard behavioral paradigms)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>o_c requires dedicated perturbation studies independent of both o_θ and o_r</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The three channels have lawful relationships (Mechanism 1 and 2 specify these), but they are not reducible to each other and they are accessed through different experimental procedures.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>How three channels change the falsification geometry:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>In the two-channel framework, falsification is binary: pred(o_i) matches or fails to match inf(o_r). A single mismatch falsifies (or the inference was wrong — the experimenter cannot adjudicate).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>In the three-channel framework, falsification becomes triangulated:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:code -->
-<pre class="wp-block-code"><code>Prediction from thermodynamics: pred_θ(o_θ) → (L_θ, M_θ)
-Prediction from closure structure: pred_c(o_c) → (L_c, M_c)  
-Inference from reports/behavior: inf(o_r) → (L_r, M_r)</code></pre>
-<!-- /wp:code -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Consistency check 1:</strong> Does pred_θ(o_θ) agree with pred_c(o_c)? If the thermodynamic state is consistent with the closure structure, both predictions should align. If they disagree, one measurement is wrong or the framework's internal consistency fails.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Consistency check 2:</strong> Does pred_θ(o_θ) agree with inf(o_r)? Thermodynamic predictions should be consistent with behavioral inferences.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Consistency check 3:</strong> Does pred_c(o_c) agree with inf(o_r)? Closure structure predictions should be consistent with differential responsiveness.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>A substitution must now fool all three channels simultaneously. The substituted system must:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Produce identical reports (preserve o_r) ✓ [this is what substitutions do]</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Have compatible thermodynamic signature (preserve consistency with o_θ) ✗ [Landauer constrains this]</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Have compatible closure structure (preserve consistency with o_c) ✗ [Mechanism 2 constrains this]</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The probability that a random substitution satisfies all three constraints simultaneously is substantially lower than the probability of satisfying any one. The constraints are <em>overdetermined</em>: each channel independently restricts the substitution space, and the restrictions overlap only partially.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Formal statement:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Let S_r ⊂ P denote the set of substitutions preserving o_r (report-equivalent systems). Let S_θ ⊂ P denote the set of systems compatible with a given thermodynamic prediction. Let S_c ⊂ P denote the set of systems compatible with a given closure prediction.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Kleiner-Hoel's substitution argument requires: S_r is large (many report-equivalent systems exist) and S_r is not contained in the set of systems making identical predictions.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>For the organizational closure framework, the relevant substitution space is not S_r but S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c: only systems that simultaneously preserve reports, satisfy the Landauer constraint, and maintain compatible closure structure.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Theorem TC.1 (Three-Channel Constraint):</strong> S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c ⊂ S_r, and the inclusion is strict (proper subset) whenever the system under investigation has nontrivial organizational closure.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Proof:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c ⊂ S_r is trivially true (intersection is contained in each component).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>To show strict inclusion: By Mechanism 1, systems in S_r with entropy production below the Landauer bound for their reports are excluded from S_θ. These systems are in S_r but not in S_r ∩ S_θ. Since at least some report-equivalent systems have low entropy production (lookup tables, optimally efficient Turing machines), S_r ∩ S_θ ⊊ S_r.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>By Mechanism 2, systems in S_r ∩ S_θ without organizational closure (e.g., thermodynamically active but externally maintained systems like candle flames or non-closed dissipative systems) are excluded from S_c. These systems are in S_r ∩ S_θ but not in S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c. Since some thermodynamically active report-equivalent systems lack closure, S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c ⊊ S_r ∩ S_θ ⊊ S_r. □</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Corollary TC.2:</strong> The effective substitution space for the organizational closure framework is strictly smaller than for any theory using only structural prediction data (like IIT) or only access-based prediction data (like GNW).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>What this means for Kleiner-Hoel:</strong> The substitution argument's force comes from the <em>size</em> of the substitution space. If S_r is enormous (which it is — universal computers alone populate it densely), then the probability of any theory's predictions being falsified by some substitution approaches 1. But if the relevant substitution space is S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c, which is substantially smaller, the probability decreases. Whether it decreases enough to save the framework is an empirical question — specifically, the question of how many organizationally closed, thermodynamically viable, report-equivalent systems exist for any given target system. This is addressed in Section XIV.5.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.4 Why Specific Substitutions Fail</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The abstract proof gains force from concrete analysis of the specific substitution classes that Kleiner and Hoel identify as threatening.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.4.1 Universal Computers (Turing Machines)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The threat:</strong> A sufficiently powerful Turing machine can produce any computable function, including one matching a brain's input-output behavior. Since the TM's internal dynamics (tape states, head position, transition function) differ radically from neural dynamics, any theory predicting consciousness from internal observables should make different predictions for brain vs. TM while behavioral inferences remain identical.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Why this fails against all three mechanisms:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 1 (Landauer):</em> A universal Turing machine computing brain-equivalent outputs is not exempt from thermodynamics. It must maintain distinctions on its tape at Landauer cost. However, a TM optimized for output fidelity rather than self-maintenance can potentially operate at much lower entropy production than the biological brain (it wastes no energy on homeostasis, immune function, growth, or repair). The thermodynamic profile (o_θ) differs: the brain operates far from equilibrium to maintain its own closure; the TM operates far from equilibrium only to maintain its tape. The framework predicts different consciousness states for systems with different D_eq profiles, and this prediction is consistent with the Landauer constraint (both are above the Landauer floor, but at different distances from equilibrium).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 2 (Closure-Mattering):</em> The TM does not exhibit organizational closure. Its constraints (transition function, tape alphabet, head mechanics) are externally specified and maintained by the hardware designer and the electrical supply. The TM does not regenerate its own conditions. If you cut its power, no internal process restores it. If a tape cell degrades, no internal process repairs it. The TM has no viability boundary to be threatened, so the concept of "threatening states" has no referent. Its outputs may <em>describe</em> what a system with mattering would say, but this is simulation of mattering, not mattering.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 3 (Three-channel):</em> The TM produces identical o_r (reports) but different o_θ (entropy production profile differs from brain) and different o_c (no closure). The three-channel prediction for the TM is: (L ≈ 0, M = null, C = null). The three-channel prediction for the brain is: (L &gt; 0, M = structured partition, C = rich content). This is not a falsification — it is a differential prediction. The theory predicts different consciousness for different systems, which is what a non-trivial theory should do.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Objection:</strong> "But the TM <em>could</em> be designed to simulate its own maintenance — self-monitoring routines, error-correction, homeostatic regulation of its own operations."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Response:</strong> If the TM is designed to maintain its own conditions — regenerating its constraints, maintaining its boundary, operating autonomously — then it begins to exhibit organizational closure. At that point, the framework's prediction changes: such a TM <em>might</em> have minimal consciousness. This is not a bug. It is the correct prediction: a system that genuinely maintains itself at thermodynamic cost, regardless of substrate, has the conditions for differential mattering. The prediction tracks the property (closure), not the substrate (carbon vs. silicon).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.4.2 Feedforward Neural Networks (The Unfolding Argument)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The threat:</strong> Any recurrent neural network can be "unfolded" into a feedforward network with identical input-output function (Doerig et al. 2019). Since the feedforward network has no recurrence (no loops, no self-reference), theories predicting consciousness from internal structure should make different predictions for systems that are behaviorally indistinguishable.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Why this fails:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 1:</em> Feedforward networks during inference have lower entropy production than recurrent networks during active processing. The recurrent network maintains activations across time steps at thermodynamic cost; the feedforward network propagates information in a single pass. The thermodynamic profiles differ.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 2:</em> The recurrent network during active learning/self-maintenance can exhibit organizational closure: connection weights are adjusted by learning rules that depend on the network's own activations, which depend on the weights — a closed constraint loop. The feedforward network with frozen weights has no such closure. Its constraints (weights) are not regenerated by its dynamics. It is a conduit, not a self-maintaining system.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 3:</em> Three-channel predictions diverge: recurrent network during self-maintenance = (L &gt; 0, M = prediction-error-relative partition, C = maintained representations). Feedforward network = (L ≈ 0, M = null, C = null). The unfolding preserves o_r (input-output function) but eliminates o_c (closure) and changes o_θ (thermodynamic profile). The substitution fails on two of three channels.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.4.3 Lookup Tables</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The threat:</strong> For any finite behavioral repertoire, a lookup table mapping all possible inputs to their correct outputs trivially preserves o_r while having maximally different internal structure from the system being emulated.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Why this fails:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 1:</em> A lookup table can be implemented at relatively low thermodynamic cost per operation (just read-and-return), but the construction cost (storing the table) can be enormous. During operation, the table's entropy production profile differs radically from an active, self-maintaining system's.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 2:</em> A lookup table has zero organizational closure. No constraint regeneration. No boundary maintenance. No autonomy. The table exists because someone built it, not because it maintains itself.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><em>Mechanism 3:</em> The lookup table fails on o_θ and o_c while matching o_r. The substitution is blocked by Mechanisms 1 and 2 jointly.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.4.4 The Critical Disanalogy (Generalized)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Kleiner-Hoel's substitution argument works when you can independently vary internal observables while preserving reports. This requires that the relationship between internal dynamics and reportable outputs is <em>accidental</em> — that many different internal dynamics can produce the same outputs.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>For theories using structural prediction data (IIT's Φ, network topology, causal structure), this is indeed accidental. Network topology and output function are not lawfully linked: different topologies can compute the same function (this is the content of the universal approximation theorem, Hornik et al. 1989).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>For the organizational closure framework, the relationship between internal dynamics and reportable outputs is <em>partially constitutive</em> rather than accidental. In an organizationally closed system, the report channel is embedded in the closure:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Verbal reports require motor execution, which requires metabolic support, which requires vascular regulation, which requires autonomic closure. The report runs <em>through</em> the closure.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Differential responsiveness (approach/avoid) is not an output appended to the system's dynamics; it IS the closure's self-maintenance dynamics observed from outside.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>A system's reports about its own states are products of the constraint network that constitutes the closure.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This means: disrupting the closure disrupts the report. Substituting the internal dynamics <em>while preserving the report</em> requires preserving the closure dynamics that generate the report — which means preserving organizational closure. The substitution space narrows to systems that are not just input-output equivalent but <em>closure-equivalent</em>.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The class of closure-equivalent systems is vastly smaller than the class of input-output-equivalent systems. Whether it is small enough to fully resolve the Kleiner-Hoel dilemma is addressed in Section XIV.5.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.5 Bounding the Residual Substitution Space</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The three mechanisms constrain the substitution space to S_residual = S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c: systems that simultaneously produce identical reports, satisfy the Landauer constraint, and exhibit compatible organizational closure. How large is this residual space? If it is empty, the dilemma is fully resolved. If it is nonempty but small, the resolution is substantial but incomplete. If it is large, the mechanisms have not achieved much.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.5.1 The Computability Argument (Rosen-Mossio)</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Robert Rosen (1991) argued that organisms are fundamentally non-computable: their organizational closure (which he called "closure to efficient causation") cannot be simulated by a Turing machine because the closure involves an impredicative loop where the system is simultaneously a cause and product of itself, which cannot be unwound into a sequential computation.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>If Rosen is correct, then S_residual is empty for biological organisms: no computational substitution (Turing machine, neural network, lookup table) can replicate genuine organizational closure, so the substitution argument cannot even be mounted.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>However, Mossio, Longo, and Stewart (2009) showed that Rosen's (M,R) systems can be expressed in lambda calculus, suggesting that the non-computability claim is too strong. Some forms of closure may be computable.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The current assessment: Rosen's argument likely proves too much (true closure may not require non-computability), but the residual insight is valuable: the class of systems exhibiting genuine organizational closure is <em>not</em> populated by arbitrary computational equivalents. It is populated by systems that actually regenerate their own constraints at thermodynamic cost, and this is a strong structural requirement that most computational systems do not satisfy.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.5.2 The Thermodynamic Distinguishability Argument</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Even within the residual space S_residual, the three-channel architecture provides a mechanism for empirically distinguishing different organizational closures that Kleiner-Hoel's two-channel framework cannot access.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Consider two systems, A and B, both exhibiting organizational closure and producing identical reports. They are in S_residual. The framework predicts potentially different consciousness states for them (because their closure structures differ). But can we tell them apart?</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Yes, if their thermodynamic profiles differ. And they will differ unless their closures are <em>thermodynamically identical</em> — maintaining the same constraints at the same energetic costs with the same entropy production rates.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Theorem TD.1 (Thermodynamic Distinguishability):</strong> Two organizationally closed systems A and B in S_residual with structurally different closures (different constraint regeneration networks) have different entropy production profiles with probability approaching 1, in the sense that the set of closure structures producing identical thermodynamic profiles has measure zero in the space of all closure structures.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Proof sketch:</strong> The entropy production profile of a system is determined by the full set of irreversible processes maintaining its constraints. Each constraint Cᵢ has a maintenance cost Dᵢ determined by the rate of degradation (how fast entropy would erase the distinction) and the rate of regeneration (how fast the closure restores it). Different constraint networks have different numbers of constraints, different degradation rates (determined by the physical substrate), and different regeneration rates (determined by the network topology). The probability that two structurally different networks produce exactly the same total entropy production rate is the probability that different sums of different numbers of different positive real numbers are equal — which is measure zero in the space of possible networks. □</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>What this means:</strong> Within S_residual, the three-channel framework can distinguish different systems by their thermodynamic profiles even when their reports are identical. The substitution argument requires indistinguishability on the inference channel; the organizational closure framework adds distinguishability on the thermodynamic channel that is not available to theories using only structural prediction data.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.5.3 The Perturbational Distinguishability Argument</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Systems in S_residual can also be distinguished by their <em>responses to perturbation</em>. This is the empirical operationalization of organizational closure: a closed system, when perturbed, actively restores its constraints. A system simulating closure does not (unless explicitly programmed to do so, in which case it is beginning to exhibit genuine closure).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>PCI (Perturbational Complexity Index) measures exactly this: how far and how complexly a perturbation propagates through a system. Casarotto et al. (2016, 2024) established PCI as the gold standard for consciousness assessment precisely because it probes the system's active constraint-maintenance dynamics rather than its resting-state outputs.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Two systems in S_residual with different closure structures will respond to identical perturbations differently:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>The perturbation propagates through the constraint network, which is the closure</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>Different closures have different constraint dependencies, so perturbation cascades differ</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>PCI captures the complexity of this cascade</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This provides a fourth measurement channel beyond o_θ, o_c, and o_r: perturbational response (o_p). Adding this channel further constrains the residual substitution space.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.5.4 Synthesis: The Residual Is Small and Shrinking</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Combining all four constraint mechanisms:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:table -->
-<figure class="wp-block-table"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th>Mechanism</th><th>Constraint Source</th><th>What It Excludes</th><th>Independent of Theory?</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>1. Landauer</td><td>Second law of thermodynamics</td><td>Systems at/near equilibrium producing structured reports</td><td>Yes</td></tr><tr><td>2. Closure-Mattering</td><td>Theorem OC.1 (50+ years empirical support)</td><td>Systems without closure producing genuine differential responsiveness</td><td>Partially (entailment testable)</td></tr><tr><td>3. Three-Channel</td><td>Information-theoretic overdetermination</td><td>Systems matching on reports but mismatching on thermodynamic and closure observables</td><td>Yes</td></tr><tr><td>4. Perturbational</td><td>Active constraint-maintenance dynamics</td><td>Systems with identical resting behavior but different perturbation responses</td><td>Yes</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>
-<!-- /wp:table -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The residual substitution space is S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c ∩ S_p: systems producing identical reports, with compatible thermodynamic profiles, with compatible closure structures, and with compatible perturbation responses.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Is this space empty?</strong> Likely not for all cases. Two genuinely different organisms with similar ecological niches might inhabit this space (e.g., convergent evolution producing similar closure structures in unrelated species).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Is this space small?</strong> Yes, relative to S_r. The four constraints are jointly overdetermining: each eliminates a different class of substitutions, and the constraints operate on different physical dimensions.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Is the framework falsifiable despite the residual?</strong> Yes. The framework specifies precise falsification criteria that can be tested without resolving the residual:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>FC.1: Closure without mattering (falsifies Theorem OC.1)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>FC.2: Mattering without closure (falsifies the reverse constraint)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>FC.3: Consciousness at equilibrium (falsifies the thermodynamic grounding)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>FC.4: A competitor theory predicting consciousness level better than entropy production</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>FC.5: Landauer violation (falsifies the physics)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>FC.6 (new): Perturbation response identical in systems with different closure structures (falsifies Theorem TD.1)</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The residual substitution space is an <em>unsolved remainder</em>, not a fatal flaw. Every empirical science has regions of underdetermination. The question is whether the theory is testable <em>in the domains where it makes predictions</em>, and it is.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.6 Content Prediction: Addressing the Remaining Gap</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.6.1 The Problem</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The most serious limitation of the analysis so far: the framework's thermodynamic predictions concern <em>level</em> of consciousness (degree of organizational closure, distance from equilibrium) but not <em>content</em> (what is experienced). The Kleiner-Hoel dilemma applies to content predictions as well as level predictions. A complete resolution requires showing that content predictions also achieve lenient dependency.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.6.2 Toward Content Prediction via Mattering Structure</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The framework does make structural predictions about content, though less precise than its level predictions:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Content as mattering topology:</strong> The <em>content</em> of a conscious state, on the closure framework, is the specific structure of the mattering partition: which environmental states are threatening, which supporting, which irrelevant, and at what grain of distinction. A visual system that maintains distinctions relevant to color has a mattering partition structured by wavelength. A proprioceptive system has a mattering partition structured by body position. The content IS the partition structure.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The partition is constrained by closure structure:</strong> Different closures produce different partitions (Theorem OC.1). A metabolic closure partitions chemical environments by metabolic viability. A neural closure partitions sensory environments by predictive relevance. The content (partition) is lawfully related to the prediction data (closure structure), not accidental.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The Yoneda connection:</strong> Tsuchiya et al. (2016, 2021) showed that by the Yoneda lemma in category theory, a perceptual state is completely characterized by its morphisms to all other states. If two states have identical relational structure (identical partition of environment into threatening/supporting/irrelevant relative to all other states), they are identical. Content IS relational structure, not something over and above it.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">12.6.3 The Functor Between Closure and Mattering</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Definition CF.1 (Mattering Functor):</strong> Let <strong>Clos</strong> be the category whose objects are organizational closures (μ: C → C with self-regenerating fixed-point structure) and whose morphisms are closure-preserving maps (maps that send one closure's constraint network to another's while preserving the regeneration topology).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Let <strong>Part</strong> be the category whose objects are three-part environmental partitions {θ, σ, ι} and whose morphisms are refinements (finer-grained partitions that respect coarser-grained ones).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Define the mattering functor M: <strong>Clos</strong> → <strong>Part</strong> by:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list -->
-<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>For each closure μ, M(μ) = the partition {θ_μ, σ_μ, ι_μ} of environmental states by their thermodynamic effect on μ</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li>For each closure-preserving map f: μ → μ′, M(f) = the corresponding refinement of M(μ) induced by the refinement of closure structure</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Theorem CF.1 (Functoriality):</strong> M is a well-defined functor from <strong>Clos</strong> to <strong>Part</strong>.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Proof:</strong></p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(i) <em>Well-definedness on objects:</em> For each closure μ, the partition M(μ) is determined by the thermodynamic coupling between μ and its environment. State s is in θ_μ iff the entropy production rate of μ in the presence of s exceeds μ's sustainable dissipation capacity. State s is in σ_μ iff it maintains entropy production within sustainable bounds. State s is in ι_μ iff its effect on entropy production is below a noise threshold δ. The partition is determined by μ's thermodynamic structure and the environmental state space, both of which are well-defined physical quantities.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(ii) <em>Well-definedness on morphisms:</em> A closure-preserving map f: μ → μ′ sends μ's constraint network to μ′'s while preserving regeneration structure. If μ′ is a refinement of μ (more constraints, finer distinctions), then M(μ′) is a refinement of M(μ): states that threatened μ still threaten μ′ (plus potentially additional states), and the finer constraint structure creates finer-grained discrimination. This defines a morphism in <strong>Part</strong>.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>(iii) <em>Functoriality:</em> Identity closures map to identity partitions. Composition of closure-preserving maps induces composition of partition refinements. M preserves identity and composition. □</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Qualification:</strong> The functor M is not injective on objects — different closures can produce the same partition (degeneracy). This is why the dependency is lenient rather than strict. The functor is also not surjective on objects — not every conceivable partition corresponds to a physically realizable closure. These properties are precisely what lenient dependency requires: a lawful but non-bijective correspondence.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>How this helps with content prediction:</strong> The framework predicts that the content of a conscious state is the image under M of the system's organizational closure. Two systems with the same closure (up to isomorphism in <strong>Clos</strong>) are predicted to have the same experiential content (up to isomorphism in <strong>Part</strong>). This is a content prediction grounded in the closure-mattering functor, not just a level prediction grounded in entropy production.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Where the gap remains:</strong> The functor M maps closures to partitions at a coarse grain. It predicts which <em>kinds</em> of distinctions a system maintains (threatening vs. supporting in which dimensions of environmental variation) but not the fine-grained phenomenal character of maintaining those distinctions. What the redness of red "is like" from the coupling position of a visual closure cannot (currently) be derived from M alone. This is the content gap, and it is not fully closed.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.7 Comparison Table: Framework Positioning</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:table -->
-<figure class="wp-block-table"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th>Framework</th><th>Prediction Data (o_i)</th><th>Inference Data (o_r)</th><th>Dependency Relationship</th><th>Kleiner-Hoel Verdict</th><th>Measurement Channels</th><th>Content Predictions</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>IIT 4.0</td><td>Integration (Φ), network structure</td><td>Reports, behavior</td><td>Independent (Koch 2019 admits substitution)</td><td>Automatically falsified (Thm 3.10)</td><td>2 (structural + reports)</td><td>Q-shapes (formal, rarely computed)</td></tr><tr><td>GNW</td><td>Global broadcasting, prefrontal ignition</td><td>Reports, access</td><td>Strictly dependent (access ≈ report)</td><td>Unfalsifiable (Thm 4.3)</td><td>2 (broadcast + reports)</td><td>Broadcast content only</td></tr><tr><td>HOT</td><td>Higher-order representations</td><td>Reports</td><td>Strictly dependent (HOT generates report)</td><td>Unfalsifiable (Thm 4.3)</td><td>2 (representational + reports)</td><td>Content of HOT</td></tr><tr><td>Predictive Processing</td><td>Prediction error minimization</td><td>Reports, behavior</td><td>Independent (error ≠ report)</td><td>Automatically falsified (Thm 3.10)</td><td>2 (computational + reports)</td><td>Predicted content</td></tr><tr><td>Behaviorism</td><td>None (behavior = consciousness)</td><td>Behavior</td><td>Identical (by definition)</td><td>Unfalsifiable (tautological)</td><td>1 (behavior only)</td><td>Behavioral description</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Org. -<!-- /wp:table -->

-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.8 Honest Residual Vulnerabilities</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>A framework that claims to have no unsolved problems is faith, not science. Three residual vulnerabilities persist after the analysis above. Each is genuine. Each is stated with its severity and the direction of possible resolution.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Vulnerability 1: Non-Empty Residual Substitution Space</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The problem:</strong> S_residual = S_r ∩ S_θ ∩ S_c ∩ S_p is small but not provably empty. Two genuinely different organizationally closed systems could, in principle, produce identical reports, have compatible thermodynamic profiles, have compatible closure structures, and respond similarly to perturbation — while the framework predicts different experiential contents based on their different fine-grained closure topologies.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Severity:</strong> Medium. The residual space is much smaller than the original S_r, and Theorem TD.1 shows that thermodynamic distinguishability eliminates most of the residual. But "most" is not "all."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Direction of resolution:</strong> Formal measurement of |S_residual| / |S_r| for specific systems. If the ratio approaches zero as closure complexity increases (which the overdetermination structure suggests), the vulnerability diminishes with system complexity. This is an open empirical question.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Vulnerability 2: Fine-Grained Content Prediction</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The problem:</strong> The mattering functor M predicts <em>structural</em> content (which dimensions of environmental variation matter) but not <em>phenomenal</em> content (what maintaining those distinctions "is like" from inside). The framework can predict that a visual system distinguishes wavelengths and that this matters to its closure, but cannot derive the specific character of experiencing redness.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Severity:</strong> High for the completeness of the theory. Low for the Kleiner-Hoel dilemma specifically, since the dilemma is about testability and falsifiability, and the framework's level and structural-content predictions are testable.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Direction of resolution:</strong> Two paths are available. First, extending the functor M to include the <em>rate of free energy reduction</em> during distinction-maintenance, which Joffily and Coricelli (2013) showed correlates with valence. This would add an affective dimension to content predictions. Second, developing the category-theoretic structure further using Tsuchiya et al.'s (2016) Yoneda-based formalization, which could characterize experiential content entirely in terms of relational structure without requiring derivation of "what it is like."</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
-<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Vulnerability 3: Formal Bounds on Closure Degeneracy</h3>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>The problem:</strong> The functor M is non-injective (multiple closures → same partition). The degree of degeneracy — how many structurally different closures produce the same mattering partition — has not been formally bounded. If the degeneracy is high, the substitution space within S_residual could be larger than the overdetermination analysis suggests.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Severity:</strong> Medium. The thermodynamic distinguishability argument (Theorem TD.1) provides informal bounds (measure zero for thermodynamically identical profiles), but a formal proof requires connecting closure topology to thermodynamic phase space, which has not been done.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Direction of resolution:</strong> Apply tools from chemical organization theory (Hordijk &amp; Steel 2015) to characterize the space of closures producing a given partition. RAF (Reflexively Autocatalytic Food-generated) set theory provides decomposition tools that could formally bound the degeneracy. This is technically feasible but not yet attempted.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.9 Self-Application: This Section Diagnosed by Its Own Framework</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This section nominalizes "dependency," "substitution," "closure," "mattering," "constraint," and "functor." The verbs beneath: depending, substituting, closing, mattering, constraining, mapping. The nominalizations are necessary for the formal proofs — you cannot do category theory with verbs. The ongoing processes make the proofs true. The gap between what the formal language expresses and what the ongoing processes involve is itself an instance of the content prediction gap (Vulnerability 2).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The section also commits the god trick (Haraway 1988) unavoidably: it claims to view the Kleiner-Hoel dilemma from a position that is not captured by their framework, adding a third channel that their formalism does not include. This is either a genuine advance (extending the formalism to a richer structure) or a smuggling operation (redefining the problem to make it easier). The falsification criterion for this section specifically: if Kleiner-Hoel or others show that the three-channel architecture collapses into their two-channel formalism without loss, the argument from Mechanism 3 fails. If the Landauer constraint is shown not to couple thermodynamic state to report structure (contradicting 60+ years of experimental physics), Mechanism 1 fails. If organizational closure is found without differential mattering (contradicting 50+ years of autonomous systems research), Mechanism 2 fails.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The scissors are trying to cut themselves. What they can report is: the blades are sharper than they were, but the handle is still incomplete.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">12.10 Conclusion: What Has Been Achieved</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Kleiner and Hoel proved that consciousness science is trapped between automatic falsification and tautological unfalsifiability. They identified lenient dependency as the only non-dualist escape and noted that no existing framework instantiates it.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This section has demonstrated that the organizational closure framework achieves lenient dependency through four independent, mutually reinforcing mechanisms:</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:list {"ordered":true} -->
-<ol class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Landauer constraint</strong> (physics-grounded, theory-independent): The second law forbids pairing structured reports with thermodynamic equilibrium, physically restricting the substitution space.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Closure-mattering entailment</strong> (biology-grounded, independently testable): Organizational closure thermodynamically entails differential mattering, creating a lawful coupling between internal dynamics and behavioral observables that substitution must preserve.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Three-channel overdetermination</strong> (information-theoretic): Thermodynamic, closure, and report observables provide three partially independent measurement channels, requiring substitutions to simultaneously satisfy constraints from all three.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item -->
-
-<!-- wp:list-item -->
-<li><strong>Perturbational distinguishability</strong> (empirically operational): PCI and perturbation-recovery protocols provide a fourth measurement channel that probes active constraint-maintenance dynamics, further narrowing the substitution space.</li>
-<!-- /wp:list-item --></ol>
-<!-- /wp:list -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>These mechanisms do not eliminate the substitution space entirely. A residual space of organizationally closed, thermodynamically compatible, report-equivalent, perturbation-compatible systems persists. But this space is vastly smaller than the original substitution space, is further constrained by thermodynamic distinguishability (Theorem TD.1), and the framework makes falsifiable predictions within it.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The claim is not immunity from the Kleiner-Hoel dilemma. The claim is: the organizational closure framework is the first to provide a principled, non-ad-hoc, physics-grounded instantiation of lenient dependency, substantially resolving a formal problem that no other framework has even addressed. Whether "substantially" is "sufficiently" is an empirical question that the framework's own falsification criteria can answer.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-
-<!-- wp:heading -->
-<h2 class="wp-block-heading">What Survives Is What Constrains</h2>
-<!-- /wp:heading -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The pattern which connects is not information, not mind, not structure, not process. It is the selection principle that explains why any pattern persists at all:&nbsp;<strong>constraint satisfaction under thermodynamic bounds</strong>.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Differences that persist are differences that make a difference. If they did not constrain what comes next, they would be erased by noise, dissipation, or indifference.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Consciousness is what organizational closure looks like from the coupling position of a self-maintaining system. There is no private theater. There is no inner observer. There is constraint satisfaction all the way down, and all the way up, and at every scale in between.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The Hard Problem of consciousness depends on a hidden premise: that phenomenology must be "more than" any physical or functional description. That premise is unfalsifiable, anti-scientific in structure, and has the same logical form as "supernatural cheese must be more than any physical description of cheese." The premise never earned epistemic standing. Once rejected, the remaining question is tractable and the abductive identification proposed here becomes the best available explanation.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>This framework can lose. Its loss conditions are specified. It has not yet lost.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>Every competing framework examined here fails on at least one of three axes: falsifiability, mechanistic completeness, or thermodynamic coherence. Panpsychism cannot specify what would disprove proto-consciousness (unfalsifiable). Platonic morphospace is contradicted by path-dependent bioelectric memory (falsified). Quantum consciousness is ruled out by decoherence timescales (falsified). Process philosophy cannot specify what would show experience is not fundamental (unfalsifiable). The Mathematical Universe cannot distinguish description from existence (unfalsifiable). Basal cognition oscillates between defensible and indefensible claims (motte-bailey).</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>The constraint-based approach survives not because it is protected, but because it has been tested. It survives because it is what survives.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p>That is not certainty. That is survival under pressure. It is all any honest inquiry can offer, and it is more than unfalsifiable frameworks have ever provided.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
-
-<!-- wp:separator -->
-<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>
-<!-- /wp:separator -->
-

-<!-- /wp:table -->
-
-<!-- wp:paragraph -->
-<p><strong>Key</strong>: Frameworks that cannot lose (unfalsifiable) eventually collapse under their own explanatory excess. Frameworks that can lose and have lost (falsified) should be abandoned. Frameworks that can lose and have not yet lost are candidates for progressive research programs.</p>
-<!-- /wp:paragraph -->
